# CLAUDE.md — Oddzilla agent doc

This file is loaded into every agent's context. Read it before touching code.
For deeper references see [`docs/`](./docs/).

## TL;DR

Oddzilla is a B2C esports sportsbook MVP. Go services handle the hot path
(Oddin AMQP feed, odds publishing, settlement, bet-delay, wallet watching).
TypeScript handles the REST API, WebSocket gateway, and Next.js frontend.
Postgres 16 + Redis 7 + Caddy, all on one Hetzner box via Docker Compose.

| Question | Answer |
| --- | --- |
| What sportsbook are we building? | B2C, esports-only. Every Oddin esport is surfaced; CS2, DOTA2, LOL, Valorant pinned at the top of the sidebar; `efootballbots` + `ebasketballbots` excluded (backend blocklist + DB inactive). |
| Which markets? | All Oddin markets — the `provider_market_id` whitelist was dropped at both feed-ingester and `/catalog/matches/:id`. Known labels for match-winner (`1`) and map-winner (`4`); others render as "Market #N". |
| Which feed? | Oddin.gg, **protocol level** (raw AMQP + REST, no SDK) for esports. Traditional sports come from the public **Fonbet** line via [`services/fonbet-ingester`](./services/fonbet-ingester/) — `fon.bet` **in English** since 2026-09-04 (was `fonbet.kz` in Russian; same line, same ids) — (HTTP poll, gated by `FONBET_ENABLED`; settlement from Fonbet's results feed via the `settlement.external` stream — see [`docs/FONBET.md`](./docs/FONBET.md)). |
| Which chains? | USDC on ERC20 (Ethereum) only. Migration 0032 renamed the currency from USDT and dropped Tron + per-user HD addresses. |
| Where does it run? | Hetzner CPX31 at `178.104.174.24` (SSH key-based access as `team`). 4 vCPU / 8 GB / 160 GB — upgraded from CPX22 on 2026-05-11 to give the storefront SSR replica fan-out (3× Next.js processes) room to breathe alongside postgres, api, and the Go workers. Public URLs: **`oddzilla.cc`** (storefront, apex) + **`sadmin.oddzilla.cc`** (admin; `/` redirects to `/admin`). The legacy `s.oddzilla.cc` host 301s to the apex via a hard-coded Caddyfile block — drop the block and DNS record once the old subdomain stops receiving traffic. Let's Encrypt via Caddy. |
| How is money stored? | `BIGINT _micro` (6 decimals = 1,000,000 micro per unit) on every wallet/ledger/ticket row, with a sibling `currency CHAR(4)`. Two currencies: `USDC` (real, on-chain via ERC20) and `OZ` (demo, every signup gets a 1000 OZ bonus for testing the bet flow). Withdrawals + deposits stay USDC-only. |
| Auth? | Email + password (argon2id), JWT access (15 min) + refresh cookie (30 d). Cookies are **host-scoped** (no `Domain` attribute) — a session on `oddzilla.cc` and a session on `sadmin.oddzilla.cc` are independent, so an operator can be logged in as a bettor on the apex and as an admin on the backoffice in the same browser. The pre-2026-05-18 model shared the cookie via `Domain=.oddzilla.cc`; the legacy cookie is killed alongside every new set so in-flight sessions migrate cleanly. **The same email may back a bettor AND an admin row** (migration 0065): email uniqueness is split into two partial indexes keyed by `users.role` (`'user'` = bettor namespace, `'admin'`/`'support'` = admin namespace), and `/auth/login` filters by the namespace implied by the request host (`oddzilla.cc` → bettor, `sadmin.oddzilla.cc` → admin) so the subdomain implicitly picks which account to authenticate. |
| Shell widths? | 240px sidebar + 900px main cap + **380px** right rail = 1520px shell max-width. The rail went 300 → 380 on 2026-09-05: 300 was sized for a rail holding the bet slip and little else, and it now also carries the Sportradar Head to Head and Live Table embeds — a ten-column league table had ~267px of usable width and grew its own horizontal scrollbar. The Live Table frame full-bleeds (cancels the section's 16px side padding) for another 32px. **380 is not a measured minimum** — the widget is cross-origin on a host the dev environment cannot load, so if the table still clips, the rail width and the shell max-width move together. |
| UI aesthetic? | Premium / quiet editorial: Instrument Serif display + Geist UI + Geist Mono for odds. Light theme default (`#f4f2ec`), dark via `data-theme="dark"` (toggle in the top bar; choice persists in `localStorage["oz:theme"]`). Ported from the Claude Design handoff bundle. **No emojis.** |

Current phase: **Phases 1–8 complete + security hardening + catalog cleanup
+ frontend redesign + USDC payments rewrite (migration 0032).** News
scraper was cancelled during Phase 8 (migration 0003). The site went
live at `s.oddzilla.cc` on 2026-04-18 and moved to the apex
`oddzilla.cc` shortly after; market whitelist active, and
`payback_margin_bp=0` globally (Oddin already sends margined odds —
see [`docs/OPERATIONS.md`](./docs/OPERATIONS.md) before re-enabling).
Migration 0032 (2026-05-07) replaced the per-user HD-address USDT
flow with a single shared ERC20 address; deposits are now attributed
by matching the on-chain sender against the bettor's signature-verified
linked wallet addresses (there is intentionally NO user-facing
paste-your-tx-hash route — that would be a deposit-theft vector), and
unlinked-sender deposits fall through to admin review. wallet-watcher
is intent-driven. Next: pre-launch
exit gates (KYC, wallet reconciliation, off-server backup rsync,
monitoring, runbook). Full roadmap in
[`docs/PHASES.md`](./docs/PHASES.md).

## Architecture map

```
Oddin AMQP+REST ── feed-ingester (Go) ──► Postgres (markets, outcomes, odds_history)
                                       └─► Redis Streams (odds.raw)

Fonbet HTTP JSON ──── fonbet-ingester (Go) ──► Postgres (same tables; fb: URNs,
   (poll every 5 s,                              provider_market_id >= 1 000 000)
    traditional sports)                     └─► Redis Streams (odds.raw)

Redis Streams ──► odds-publisher (Go) ──► Postgres (published_odds)
                                       └─► Redis pub/sub (odds:match:{id})

Redis pub/sub ──► ws-gateway (TS) ──► browsers (WebSocket, 1 MiB/socket buffer cap)
                                       │
                                       └─ also user:{id} channels for ticket frames

Browser ── Caddy (lb_policy least_conn) ──► web1 / web2 / web3 (Next.js SSR)
                                                  │
                                                  ▼
                                              api (TS Fastify) ──► Postgres
                                                  │
                                                  ├─► POST /bets → ticket → pg_notify('bet_delay')
                                                  │      ▼
                                                  │   bet-delay (Go) ──► Postgres (promote/reject)
                                                  │
                                                  └─► POST /wallet/withdrawals → locks stake → admin queue

Oddin AMQP ──► settlement (Go) ──► Postgres (settlements + tickets + wallet_ledger)
                                    └─► Redis pub/sub (user:{id} ticket frames)

Oddin Bifrost GraphQL ──wss──► bifrost-feed (Go) ──► Redis stream `oddin.backup`
  (backup; standby until feed-ingester's        ├─► feed-ingester (odds_change, fixture_change)
   `feed:primary:last_msg_unix` stamp is stale) └─► settlement    (bet_settlement)
  feed-ingester automap: REST fixture fails ──► Bifrost `match` query (same FixtureResponse shape)

Tron + ETH RPC ──► wallet-watcher (Go) ──► Postgres (deposits + wallet_ledger)
                                            (per-chain block scanner + confirmations
                                             tick + atomic credit)

Browser ── /widgets/* (api proxy, x-brand-token) ──► Oddin Disir REST
                                                       └─► returns iframe URL for
                                                           prematch / live widgets

Browser ── /video/match/:id (api) ──► Oddin /v1/catalog (Redis 60s, one call
   │                                   covers every match: "is there a stream?")
   └─► @oddin-gg/havik-player in-page ──► *.oddin-video.gg
         (no iframe; LL-HLS + Widevine/FairPlay direct from the browser using
          the publishable key the api handed it. feed=playback+catalog,
          playback=manifest/segments, drm=licence, events.feed=SSE, beacons=QoE)

support-ai-bot (IN the stack since 2026-09-01; outbound-only, listens on nothing):
  support-ai-bot ──────► api:3001 /webhooks/support-ai/<secret>
                    └──► hosted OpenAI-compatible LLM (LM_STUDIO_BASE_URL)

Operator PC (NOT in the stack; dials OUT only, server never connects in):
  zillaboost-banner-gen ► api /webhooks/banner-gen/<secret>  ──► LM Studio + ComfyUI 127.0.0.1:8188
                          (poll queue → render → upload image)
    ^ stays on the PC permanently: ComfyUI is unauthenticated admin-level
      code execution and must remain bound to loopback. support-ai-bot was
      only ever there because its model was local; a hosted endpoint removed
      that anchor, so it moved to the box and now answers 24/7.

External SMTP ── port 25 ──► mail-receiver (Go) ──► api /webhooks/sendgrid-inbound
                                                     └─► email_inbound / email_threads
                                                         (backoffice inbox)
```

See [`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`](./docs/ARCHITECTURE.md) for the full picture, and
[`docs/ODDIN.md`](./docs/ODDIN.md) for the Oddin protocol details.

## Tech stack (locked)

- **Go 1.23** — `services/{feed-ingester, fonbet-ingester, odds-publisher, settlement, bet-delay, wallet-watcher, signer, bifrost-feed}`.
  Each service is its own Go module. Shared libs across services:
  `rabbitmq/amqp091-go`, `encoding/xml`, `jackc/pgx/v5` + `pgxpool`,
  `redis/go-redis/v9`, `rs/zerolog`. wallet-watcher uses stdlib `net/http`
  for both Ethereum JSON-RPC and TronGrid REST — no go-ethereum or
  gotron-sdk dependency for read-only scanning. Address derivation is
  done in TS (`services/api/src/lib/hdwallet.ts`); signing for
  withdrawals is intentionally not implemented yet (manual broadcast
  via admin UI for MVP).
- **Node 22 / TypeScript** — `services/{api, ws-gateway}`, `apps/web`.
  Fastify 5, `ws`, `ioredis`, `pino`, `zod`, `jose`, `@node-rs/argon2`,
  `ethers` v6 + `bs58` (HD derivation in API), Next.js 15.x, Tailwind v4,
  `qrcode.react`. shadcn/ui not yet adopted — primitives are inline in the
  dark theme; switch when a real component library is needed. The api
  container starts via `tsx src/server.ts` rather than compiled JS because
  the `@oddzilla/db` workspace ships source TypeScript; switch to a build
  step when packages get heavier.
- **Postgres 16** (extensions `pgcrypto`, `citext`; note `pg_partman` is
  NOT installed in the image despite migration 0001 trying for it — odds_history
  partition retention runs via the `oddzilla-odds-retention` cron instead, see
  `docs/OPERATIONS.md`), **Redis 7**,
  **Caddy 2**. All via Docker Compose.
- **Drizzle ORM** owns the schema. Hand-written SQL migrations in
  `packages/db/migrations/`. Go services read the same tables via raw pgx
  queries (sqlc was planned but not adopted — the query surface is small
  enough that hand-written SQL is clearer than codegen).

## Invariants (non-negotiable)

These rules are load-bearing. Breaking them causes money or data loss.

1. **Money is `BIGINT _micro` (6 decimals) per currency.**
   Column suffix is `_micro`. Never `NUMERIC` for balances/stakes. Never
   `number` in TS (precision loss > 2^53). Use the `MicroUsdt` branded bigint
   from `packages/types/src/money.ts` for arithmetic (the math is currency-
   agnostic — OZ also has 6 decimals). JSON serialization: bigints go over
   the wire as decimal strings (`"10000000"` not `10000000`).

   Every wallet read/write is scoped by `(user_id, currency)` since
   migration 0014. Forgetting the `currency` filter on a `wallets` /
   `wallet_ledger` query will silently touch the wrong row. The supported
   set is hard-coded in
   [`packages/types/src/currencies.ts`](./packages/types/src/currencies.ts)
   (`SUPPORTED_CURRENCIES = ["USDC", "OZ"]`). Withdrawals + on-chain
   deposits hard-code `currency='USDC'`; OZ has no chain.

2. **Specifier canonicalization.** A market is uniquely keyed by
   `(match_id, provider_market_id, specifiers_hash)`. The hash is sha256 of
   `k1=v1|k2=v2` with keys sorted lexicographically. Four implementations
   that MUST stay byte-identical:
   - TS: [`packages/types/src/specifiers.ts`](./packages/types/src/specifiers.ts)
   - Go (feed-ingester): `services/feed-ingester/internal/oddinxml/specifiers.go`
   - Go (settlement): `services/settlement/internal/oddinxml/specifiers.go`
     (intentionally duplicated per the per-service-Go-module rule)
   - Go (fonbet-ingester): `services/fonbet-ingester/internal/specifiers/specifiers.go`
     (same rule; the Fonbet grader also emits canonical specifiers on the
     `settlement.external` stream, which the settlement copy re-hashes)

   All four are tested against the shared
   [`docs/fixtures/specifiers.json`](./docs/fixtures/specifiers.json)
   golden table. If they diverge, settlement silently fails to match
   tickets to settled markets.

3. **Apply-once settlement.** Every Oddin settlement/cancel/rollback message
   inserts into `settlements` keyed by
   `(event_urn, market_id, specifiers_hash, type, payload_hash)` with
   `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`. If no row is returned, it's a replay — ack AMQP
   and move on. Implemented in
   `services/settlement/internal/store/store.go` `InsertIfNew()`.
   Retention boundary (2026-07-03): the dedup only needs to outlive the
   identical-replay window — AMQP redelivery plus the `RecoveryWindowCap`
   24 h recovery clamp — so the nightly `oddzilla-settlements-retention`
   cron deletes `settle`/`cancel` rows after 45 days (open-ticket-guarded;
   `rollback_*` rows kept forever since a re-applied rollback is the one
   replay class that would claw back a real payout). Bettor-facing ticket
   history (`tickets`, `ticket_selections`, `wallet_ledger`,
   `market_outcomes.result`) is never deleted by anything. A late
   CORRECTION from Oddin carries a different `payload_hash` and is meant
   to apply even with the old row present. See docs/OPERATIONS.md →
   "settlements retention".

4. **Apply-once wallet credits.** `wallet_ledger` has a unique partial index
   on `(type, ref_type, ref_id) WHERE ref_id IS NOT NULL`. Every credit
   (deposit, payout, refund, manual void, withdrawal debit) supplies a
   stable `ref_id` (ticket UUID, deposit UUID, withdrawal UUID, etc.).
   Replays are no-ops at the row level — three layers of defense
   (settlement insert, wallet_ledger unique index, transactional updates).

5. **Drizzle is the schema source of truth.** Schema changes start in
   `packages/db/src/schema/*.ts` AND a hand-written SQL file in
   `packages/db/migrations/`, with an entry appended to
   `migrations/meta/_journal.json`. Go services use raw pgx queries that
   read those tables directly — no codegen.

6. **No localhost in code.** Every inter-service URL/host comes from env.
   `packages/config/src/env.ts` parses with zod and fails fast on missing
   required vars.

7. **Redis Streams = internal bus. Redis pub/sub = best-effort fanout.**
   Postgres `published_odds` is always the source of truth when a WS client
   reconnects. Pub/sub may drop; streams do not. Two pub/sub channel
   namespaces in use:
   - `odds:match:{matchId}` — one publisher (`odds-publisher`), fan-out
     via `ws-gateway`. Not rate-limited: a sportsbook cannot drop price
     ticks, and the outbound token bucket that used to be here was
     removed in `c005882`.
   - `user:{userId}` — two publishers (`api` on placement, `bet-delay` /
     `settlement` on lifecycle), fan-out unrate-limited (low volume,
     high-value-to-user ticket frames).

   **The fanout is bounded by buffer, not by rate.** `ws.send()` never
   blocks — a consumer that stops draining (throttled phone, laptop
   asleep on a half-open TCP connection still reporting `readyState ===
   OPEN`, OS-frozen tab) has every subsequent frame queued in the
   sender, reachable, and therefore un-collectable. That is unbounded
   heap growth keyed to FEED VOLUME rather than client count, and it
   OOM-killed the 256 MiB gateway four times on 2026-09-03 while it
   served a couple of dozen sockets. Every fan-out send goes through
   `sendToClient()` in
   [`services/ws-gateway/src/server.ts`](./services/ws-gateway/src/server.ts),
   which disconnects a socket past `WS_MAX_BUFFERED_BYTES` (1 MiB)
   rather than queueing behind it. Disconnecting is the correct
   remedy precisely because of the first line of this invariant: the
   browser reconnects, resubscribes, and re-reads current prices from
   Postgres. Silently dropping frames instead would leave that client
   quoting a price the book has already moved off, with no signal.

   **A WS socket's identity is fixed at upgrade time.** ws-gateway reads
   the `oddzilla_access` cookie once, during the HTTP upgrade, and never
   re-reads it — so a socket opened while logged out never joins its
   `user:{id}` channel, no matter what happens afterwards in the tab.
   Signing in is a client-side route change, so nothing reopens it on
   its own. [`ws-session-sync.tsx`](./apps/web/src/lib/ws-session-sync.tsx)
   compares the gateway's `hello.userId` against the SSR-resolved
   session and reconnects on a mismatch (bounded — an expired cookie
   must not spin). Anything that depends on a `user:{id}` frame needs a
   transport-independent fallback anyway; see the ticket poll in
   [`bet-slip-rail.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/shell/bet-slip-rail.tsx).

   **A stream consumer must survive losing its consumer group.** Production
   Redis is `maxmemory 256mb` + `allkeys-lru`, so it can evict a stream key
   under pressure, and evicting a stream destroys its consumer groups. The
   producer's next XADD recreates the key but nothing recreates the group,
   so any consumer that only calls `XGROUP CREATE` at boot backs off on
   `NOGROUP` forever. That is what emptied every price on the storefront for
   92 minutes on 2026-09-03. Every XREADGROUP loop must detect `NOGROUP` and
   re-run its group creation inline — see `isNoGroupErr` in
   [`services/odds-publisher/internal/bus/consumer.go`](./services/odds-publisher/internal/bus/consumer.go)
   and the same branch in settlement's
   [`internal/extstream`](./services/settlement/internal/extstream/consumer.go)
   (the `settlement.external` consumer, which also creates its group from
   `0` rather than `$` so a recreate replays what is still on the stream
   instead of silently skipping unapplied settlements).
   Same lesson as the feed-source keys: Redis is a cache, not state.

   **Stream caps are a Redis-memory budget, not a burst buffer.** `odds.raw`
   is trimmed to 100k entries by BOTH producers (feed-ingester and
   fonbet-ingester must use the same value — MAXLEN is applied by whichever
   XADD runs); `settlement.external` to 20k. A producer with more to say than
   the consumer drains (fonbet-ingester's ~200k-price cold start) paces itself
   on the consumer group's lag (`bus.OddsBacklog` + `ingest.waitForOddsBacklog`,
   high-water 50k, bounded wait) instead of raising the cap: the first cut
   raised it to 400k, which at ~300 B per entry is half of the 256 MB
   `maxmemory` on its own.

8. **No emojis anywhere — UI text, logs, commit messages, code comments.**
   User requirement.

8a. **No data-provider names in bettor-facing UI.** Sportradar, Oddin,
   Fonbet and Bifrost are suppliers, and which supplier is behind a
   widget, a price or a stream is a backend fact — the storefront
   presents them as Oddzilla's own surfaces. The Sportradar chips beside
   the tracker / Head to Head / Live Table titles and the "Oddin HD"
   stream tab were removed on 2026-09-05 for exactly this reason; keep
   new widgets to a plain feature title. `/privacy` named Oddin too,
   under "Who we share it with" — that bullet was removed the same day
   because it was FACTUALLY WRONG, not merely off-brand: no bettor data
   reaches Oddin. We consume their feed and proxy their widget calls
   server-side; the only thing that ever touches them from a bettor is
   an HTTP request their embed makes, which is what the third-party
   media note covers, now phrased generically ("statistics and
   match-tracker widgets served by third-party data providers") so it
   also covers Sportradar, which it previously missed. Keep the real
   processors named there — Hetzner, Resend, Google/Firebase and the
   streaming platforms DO receive user data, and naming them is the
   disclosure's whole function. The one thing outside our reach is
   branding drawn INSIDE a third-party iframe. Admin pages are
   unaffected — an operator needs to know which feed a row came from.

9. **`markets.status` terminal codes (`-3` settled / `-4` cancelled) are
   sticky.** Once the settlement service flips a row to `-3` or `-4`, no
   subsequent `odds_change` from feed-ingester may revert it. Oddin's
   recovery snapshots replay older market state (a product=1 prematch
   snapshot received after an in-band `bet_settlement` will carry the
   same market with `status="1"` and an older `timestamp`); without this
   guard, settled markets get resurrected in the catalog with
   `market_outcomes.result` already decided — bettable at stale odds
   with a known winner. Enforced via the `CASE WHEN markets.status IN
   (-3,-4) THEN markets.status ELSE EXCLUDED.status END` branch on the
   `ON CONFLICT` clause in both [`UpsertMarket`](./services/feed-ingester/internal/store/markets.go)
   and `UpsertMarketsBulk`. Rollbacks (`rollback_bet_settlement`) go
   through `settlement`'s direct `SetMarketStatus` UPDATE (not the
   feed-ingester upsert path), so they correctly re-activate the row.
   Second-layer guard: bet placement
   ([`services/api/src/modules/bets/service.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/bets/service.ts))
   additionally rejects any leg with `market_outcomes.result IS NOT NULL`
   (`outcome_already_settled`), so a future desync between `markets.status`
   and `outcomes.result` can't be exploited to place at stale odds with
   a known winner.

10. **A provider may only ever flush its own catalog.** Every
   catalog-wide suspend must be scoped to the provider that owns the rows
   — `matches.provider_urn LIKE 'od:match:%'` for Oddin,
   `'fb:match:%'` for Fonbet. Two feeds now write the same
   `markets` / `market_outcomes` / `matches` tables, so an unscoped
   `WHERE markets.status = 1 AND ma.status IN ('not_started','live')`
   takes the OTHER provider's entire offer down as collateral.

   This is not theoretical. On 2026-09-04 the alive watchdog ran
   `store.FlushAndSuspendActiveCatalog` — correctly, from its
   forced-Backup branch, because bifrost-feed briefly went unhealthy —
   and that flush predated the second provider and matched on match
   status alone. It suspended 116 079 markets and nulled 1 976 370
   outcome prices — about 104 000 of those markets Fonbet's — and every
   traditional sport went dark while Oddin's own esports offer stayed up
   on the backup feed. Note what the trigger was NOT: any watchdog fire
   does this, from either branch, so it needed no Oddin outage at all —
   a momentary blip in whichever source is currently feeding the
   catalogue was enough. Scoped now in both copies of that flush: the Go one in
   [`services/feed-ingester/internal/store/recovery.go`](./services/feed-ingester/internal/store/recovery.go)
   (all three steps — markets, outcomes, AND the `live` → `suspended`
   match flip) and the admin `POST /admin/feed/recovery` twin in
   [`services/api/src/modules/admin/feed.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/feed.ts).

   **The second half of the invariant is recovery.** A delta-based
   ingester cannot see a write it did not make: fonbet-ingester diffs each
   snapshot against an in-memory picture of what it last wrote, so with
   its own view still saying `status = 1` it had nothing to re-assert and
   the offer stayed dark until the container was restarted by hand. Any
   such service needs a divergence check —
   `Ingester.ReconcileExternalSuspend` in
   [`services/fonbet-ingester/internal/ingest/ingest.go`](./services/fonbet-ingester/internal/ingest/ingest.go)
   runs one `COUNT` a minute against `store.CountActiveProviderMarkets`
   and, when the DB holds under half the markets it believes are active,
   mirrors the flip in memory so the next cycle re-upserts everything
   the feed still quotes. Scoping the flush removes the known trigger;
   this bounds every unknown one (the admin endpoint, hand-run SQL, a
   third provider) to a minute of dark odds instead of until the next
   restart.

## Where things live

| Concern | Path |
| --- | --- |
| Fonbet sports feed (second provider) | [`services/fonbet-ingester/`](./services/fonbet-ingester/) — `internal/fonbet` (client + factor catalogue index), `internal/mapper` (snapshot → matches / markets / specifiers / outcome ids), `internal/ingest` (diff against the previous snapshot, pg + Redis writes, staleness suspend), `internal/store` + `internal/bus` (copies of the feed-ingester adapters, `provider='fonbet'`, `fb:` URNs). Storefront hook: `loadMatchWinnerOdds` in [`services/api/src/modules/catalog/routes.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/catalog/routes.ts) accepts Fonbet match-winner rows (`provider_market_id >= 1_000_000` + outcome ids `1/2/3`). **Site + language are a matched set of env vars, and `FONBET_LANG` is not display-only.** Defaults read `fon.bet` in English (`FONBET_SITE_ORIGIN` / `FONBET_URLS_JSON` / `FONBET_LINE_HOSTS` / `FONBET_COMMON_HOSTS` / `FONBET_LOGO_CDN` / `FONBET_SCOPE_MARKET=1600`); the Kazakhstan estate `fonbet.kz` needs all six moved together — `scopeMarket` is per site (each 404s on the other's value) and the static host lists are the trust anchor for `urls.json` discovery, so a half-move rejects every discovered host. The two sites are the SAME line: measured 2026-09-04, they share the event id space, the 965-node sports tree with the same 32 root ids, and byte-identical catalogues (580 tables, identical match-winner / param-kind / outcome-id derivation per factor), so switching site or language changes **no** `provider_market_id`, `outcome_id` or `specifiers_hash` and open tickets keep resolving. What language DOES change is the settlement grader's input: `internal/settle` reads the catalogue table names, the sub-event labels and the results feed (`locale`) in `FONBET_LANG`, and `rules.go` carries English + Russian vocabularies only — see the grader row below. **Tournament marks have TWO upstream sources and neither is a superset**, merged in [`ingest.ApplyLogos`](./services/fonbet-ingester/internal/ingest/logos.go) with `line/logos` winning: that endpoint's `competitions` map (keyed by segment id, so it joins straight onto `fb:tournament:<id>`), plus [`fonbet.Client.TournamentIcons`](./services/fonbet-ingester/internal/fonbet/logos.go), which reads the `tournamentInfos` block off the `events/list` snapshot we already fetch — a different asset tree (`/Logotypes/Tournament/` vs `/Logotypes/CompetitionLogos/`) and therefore free of an extra request. Widening the first source is NOT the lever: `competitions:"all"` returns 125 593 mappings against `"actual"`'s 767 and adds exactly **zero** logos, because every id we hold is already in the narrow answer — usually as the literal `"none"`. **Fonbet simply has no mark for about half its leagues** (measured 2026-09-05: 384 real marks over 761 live segments; England's Championship, League 1, League 2 and League Cup all return `"none"`) and falls back to the country flag — fon.bet's own Championship page draws `NewFlags/Circle/England.svg`. That fallback is deliberately NOT imported (`isFlagPath` drops it): the sidebar groups tournaments under a category header that already carries the country flag, so it would stamp the same flag down a whole England bucket. A logo-less tournament keeps `logo_url` NULL and renders no mark — see the sidebar row below for how the row still stays aligned. Protocol + mapping in [`docs/FONBET.md`](./docs/FONBET.md). |
| Fonbet settlement grader — per-language vocabulary | [`services/fonbet-ingester/internal/settle/rules.go`](./services/fonbet-ingester/internal/settle/rules.go) + [`settle.go`](./services/fonbet-ingester/internal/settle/settle.go). The grader reads Fonbet text, so switching `FONBET_LANG` is a settlement change, not a display change — running the Russian word lists against the English catalogue (measured on the live catalogue 2026-09-04) left three gradable-by-shape tables unguarded ("Total missed penalties", two "Team total missed penalties"), which `Grade` would have settled off the goal score, and dropped the `"extra time"` / `"penalty shootouts"` results rows so a basketball two-way market would have graded on regular time. Three vocabularies, both languages: (1) `unsafeTableWords` / `unsafePrefixWords` — the names `Grade` must refuse; the two lists now flag identical sets of gradable-by-shape tables in either direction, pinned by `TestTableUnsafeEnglish`. (2) `periodRe` + `periodEnPrefixRe` / `periodEnSuffixRe` — English marks the period at EITHER end of a label ("1st half corners" but "Yellow cards — 1st half"), and its unit list is only what the line emits (half / period / set / quarter / inning / map); plural "innings" is excluded because Fonbet uses it for a cumulative "first N innings" row. (3) `overtimeRows` / `shootoutRows` — the tie-breaking results rows. **English "Nth half" is ambiguous and is resolved per sport**, not guessed: Russian separates `тайм` (one period) from `половина` (two periods), English says "1st half" for both, so `sportRule.half` maps football / futsal / handball / rugby → period N and basketball / american football → periods 2N-1 + 2N, and every other sport REFUSES the label (`ambiguous half label`) rather than settle on a guess. Aggregate specials stay out of scope in both languages by the same mechanism they always did — "8 matches 1st half" parses a period in English but leaves a statistic name the results feed does not carry, so `scoreFor` reports no score. Coverage measured by pairing every live sub-event label across the two languages by event id: 82 label kinds / 5 574 events grade in both, 0 grade to a DIFFERENT score, and the only one-directional gap is football's "hit the woodwork" (Fonbet's own English line and English results feed spell that row differently, so 23 events stay open for manual settlement — deliberately not papered over with fuzzy matching, since on a payout path a missed row costs a manual settlement and a wrong match costs money). Same pass fixed a pre-existing RUSSIAN gap: `серия` was a whole word, so inflected playoff-series markets ("Победа в серии" / "Фора серии" / "Тотал серии") were gradable off one match's score; it is now the prefix `сери`. |
| Oddin BetBuilder (OBB) gRPC client | [`services/api/src/lib/obb-client.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/obb-client.ts) — wraps the vendored `proto/obb/*.proto` (`AvailableMarkets` / `SessionCreate` / `SessionInfo`) with a singleton gRPC channel. Per-RPC `token` metadata key carries the Oddin access token. Graceful-idle: when `ODDIN_OBB_HOST` is empty the factory returns null and `/betbuilder/*` 503s `betbuilder_disabled`. |
| BetBuilder routes (storefront → OBB proxy) | [`services/api/src/modules/betbuilder/routes.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/betbuilder/routes.ts) — `GET /betbuilder/match/:id/markets` (toggle visibility probe), `POST /betbuilder/match/:id/quote` (SessionCreate; returns combined session odds + OBB-eligible markets, mapped back to internal market ids via `(provider_market_id, specifiers_hash)` so the slip can render against existing buttons). |
| BetBuilder placement (POST /bets) | [`services/api/src/modules/bets/service.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/bets/service.ts) — `betType="betbuilder"` + `betBuilder` block (sessionId / expectedOddsX10000 / selectionIds). Server bypasses the same-match guard, asserts every leg comes from one match, calls OBB `SessionInfo` to revalidate, then debits stake. Frozen `BetBuilderMeta` lands on `tickets.bet_meta`. |
| BetBuilder toggle (match page) | [`apps/web/src/components/match/betbuilder-toggle.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/match/betbuilder-toggle.tsx) — sport-gated (CS2 / Valorant / eFootball / eBasketball per OBB doc Appendix #1) and silently hidden when `/betbuilder/match/:id/markets` 503/404s. Toggling ON puts the slip in `mode: "betbuilder"` for that match; off reverts to combo/single. |
| BetBuilder slip mode | [`apps/web/src/lib/bet-slip.tsx`](./apps/web/src/lib/bet-slip.tsx) — `mode: "betbuilder"` plus `betbuilderMatchId` + `betbuilderQuote`. While in builder mode `add()` accepts multiple legs from the SAME match (skipping the default same-match replace); picking a leg from a different match drops every builder leg and reverts to combo. |
| BetBuilder rail rendering | [`apps/web/src/components/shell/bet-slip-rail.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/shell/bet-slip-rail.tsx) — re-quotes `/betbuilder/match/:id/quote` whenever the leg set changes, shows the combined session odds, and submits with the `betBuilder` block. |
| BetBuilder settlement payout | [`services/settlement/internal/settler/payout.go`](./services/settlement/internal/settler/payout.go) `BetBuilderPayout` — all-won → `stake × oddsX10000/10_000` (bet_payout); any leg lost → 0; any leg voided + rest non-loss → refund stake (bet_refund) since OBB combined odds aren't a product and we can't recompute on partial voids. |
| OBB proto schema (vendored from `oddin-gg/obbschema`) | [`services/api/proto/obb/`](./services/api/proto/obb/) — `service.proto`, `session.proto`, `markets.proto`, `popular.proto`. Loaded at runtime via `@grpc/proto-loader`; not committed as generated code. |
| RiskZilla engine (TS) | [`services/api/src/lib/riskzilla/engine.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/riskzilla/engine.ts) — runs inside the bet placement transaction, evaluates min stake, max payout, per-tier match liability cap, per-bettor slice (bet_factor × match_cap × RS, ceilinged at the absolute match cap so high RS can't exceed everyone-combined; no automatic damping — operator dials RS down explicitly), bank cap, and per-market factor; on accept bumps `riskzilla_bank_state.open_liability_micro` and writes `riskzilla_event_log`; on reject the placement tx rolls back, then BetsService writes the event_log row outside the rolled-back tx and throws a typed `BadRequestError`. **Combo liability attribution uses NW-share**: each leg's contribution to its (match, market, outcome) bucket is `ticket_payout × (odds_i − 1) / Σ(odds_j − 1)` (model from `HumanDocs/Exhibit 2 - Multibets.xlsx`). A tier-1 match in a combo with a tier-9 partner no longer gets the FULL combo payout charged against its tight cap — only its NW share. Historical bucket sums (other open tickets) are share-weighted on-the-fly via a `leg_shares` CTE so old + new bets stay consistent. Applies to the per-match liability cap gate AND the per-bettor cap gate. **Bank cap is intentionally NOT share-weighted** — it's a hard solvency check against the operator's real crypto cash, and a winning combo pays the full express. `open_liability_micro` (the bank-side counter) tracks the FULL ticket potential payout per ticket for the same reason: if every open ticket wins, the operator owes the sum of full payouts. **Bank gate is the strict version**: `bet_max_loss + open_liability + Σ wallet.balance_micro (USDC) ≤ bank_limit_micro`. Bettor wallet balances are withdrawable on demand and count against the operator's risk capital — accepting a bet that would push committed capital (balances + open potential payouts + this bet) over the bank is rejected with `bank_limit_exceeded` even if the simpler `open_liability + bet ≤ bank` form would otherwise pass. The dashboard + bank pages surface the breakdown as `Bank / Bettor balances / Open liability / Free capacity` with `Free` going red when the operator is over-committed. **`bank_limit_micro` is the operator's REAL crypto-account cash** (seed + Σ credited deposits − Σ confirmed withdrawals + admin manual adjustments) post-migration 0050 — bet outcomes don't move it, only deposits/withdrawals/manual adjustments do. `services/settlement/internal/store/store.go` `UpdateRiskzillaBankOnSettle` / `UpdateRiskzillaBankOnReverse` decrement / re-add `open_liability_micro` on every settle / cancel / rollback (no longer touch `bank_limit_micro`). **Settlement is only one of four exit paths that must release the counter** — `maybeSettleTicket` gates on `status='accepted'`, so it never runs for tickets that left the open set another way. The other three each release inline: the bet-delay worker's `RejectAndRefund` (Go), and — via the shared `releaseOpenLiability()` helper in [`services/api/src/lib/riskzilla/open-liability.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/riskzilla/open-liability.ts) — cashout accept ([`cashout/service.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/cashout/service.ts), `cashed_out`) and admin manual void ([`admin/tickets.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/tickets.ts), `voided`). Forgetting any of these leaks the ticket's full potential payout into the cached counter until `/admin/riskzilla/bank/recompute` rebuilds it from open tickets. **Every writer of `open_liability_micro` must be USDC-gated in BOTH directions** — the engine bypasses OZ entirely, so an unguarded *increment* injects a phantom balance that no decrement can ever remove (the decrements are themselves USDC-gated, and a recompute only sums USDC open tickets). The sixth writer, bet-delay's `AcceptWithUpdatedOdds` re-price delta, was missing that guard until 2026-08-28 and had accumulated 8.5 "USDC" of fictional open liability from OZ re-prices; the counter is CHAR(4)-padded so trim before comparing (`"OZ  "` vs `"USDC"`). Wallet-watcher `CreditIntent` and admin `/admin/deposits/:id/credit-manual` insert `deposit_credit` ledger rows + bump `bank_limit_micro` on credit; admin `/admin/withdrawals/:id/mark-confirmed` inserts a `withdrawal_debit` ledger row + decrements `bank_limit_micro` by `amount` (the `fee` stays in the operator's bank as profit). |
| RiskZilla admin routes (TS) | [`services/api/src/modules/admin/riskzilla/routes.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/riskzilla/routes.ts) — sub-modules `settings`, `bank`, `bettors`, `events`, `dashboard`, `live-delay` (migration 0052 — global / sport / tournament / match cascade override of the live bet acceptance delay; per-leg `match > tournament > sport > global` with MAX across live legs, then MAX with `users.bet_delay_seconds`; **pure-prematch placements get zero delay** — the per-user knob ONLY applies when at least one leg is live; consumed in [`services/api/src/modules/bets/service.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/bets/service.ts) at placement time). Bank edit (`PUT /admin/riskzilla/bank/limit`) and `/admin/riskzilla/bank/recompute` are extra-gated to a single admin email (`q1qooo@gmail.com`) — anyone else with admin role gets a `bank_admin_only` 403. Recompute rebuilds both `open_liability_micro` (sum of open tickets' potential_payout) AND `bank_limit_micro` from ledger rows (`seed + deposit_credit + withdrawal_debit + manual_adjust where ref_type IS NULL or != 'ticket'` — excludes legacy bet rows and settlement-reverse rows). Every mutation writes `admin_audit_log`. |
| RiskZilla admin pages (web) | [`apps/web/src/app/admin/riskzilla/`](./apps/web/src/app/admin/riskzilla/) — `page.tsx` (dashboard KPIs + bank utilisation + top exposure by match + bettor RS histogram), `settings/`, `market-factors/`, `live-delay/` (lazy-loaded sport → tournament → match tree; each row shows its own override or the effective inherited value + source, with per-row Save / Clear; global row seeds at 5s and cannot be deleted), `bank/`, `betticker/` (live tail polling every 3 s with status + reason filter pills + pause; the Status column is the ticket's CURRENT lifecycle state derived in `events.ts`, not the frozen `riskzilla_event_log.decision` — including `rejected_delay` for a bet-delay rejection, which RiskZilla itself recorded as `accepted`, so the generic Rejected filter overlays ticket state too), `bets/` (historical search w/ date-range + cursor pagination), `bettors/` + `bettors/[id]/` (list + per-bettor profile with RS editor). Sidebar entry under **Risk & limits → RiskZilla**. |
| RiskZilla bot controls (placement intent + minimum human time + velocity caps + behaviour scoring) | **Premise (2026-09-03): bet placement cannot be restricted to a physical mouse click** — the server only sees HTTP, Playwright drives a real browser with `isTrusted` clicks, and any "click-only" gate also locks out keyboard and screen-reader users. These controls instead make automation gain nothing and get noticed. **Placement intent** — [`services/api/src/modules/bets/intent.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/bets/intent.ts): `POST /bets/intent` (auth, 120/min) mints a stateless HMAC token (key derived from `JWT_SECRET`) bound to `(user, sorted selection set, issued-at, nonce)`; `POST /bets` requires it when `riskzilla_bot_controls.intent_required` is on (default), rejecting `intent_required` / `intent_invalid` / `intent_selection_mismatch` / `intent_expired` / `intent_too_fast` / `intent_replayed`. The token binds the selection SET, not the odds — binding odds would reset the human-time clock on every live tick and starve a live slip; odds stay validated by the existing authoritative-price + drift path. Single-use is best-effort via a Redis nonce keyed to the placement's idempotency key (a network retry of the same placement passes; Redis down = allow, the signature + TTL + human-time checks still hold). The slip hook [`apps/web/src/lib/use-bet-intent.ts`](./apps/web/src/lib/use-bet-intent.ts) quotes on every selection-set change (debounced), refreshes 15 s before expiry, and [`bet-slip-rail.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/shell/bet-slip-rail.tsx) waits out the remaining `min_human_ms` before posting and re-quotes + retries once on a stale token, so bettors never see these codes. **Minimum human time** — `min_human_ms` (default 600) measured server-side from the token's issued-at; a bot can wait, and waiting is exactly what kills the latency-arbitrage edge. Every ticket records `quote_to_place_ms`. **Velocity caps** — `RiskzillaEngine.evaluateVelocity` in [`engine.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/riskzilla/engine.ts), called from `bets/service.ts` for EVERY currency (unlike `evaluate()`, which fences OZ) while the user row is held FOR UPDATE: placements in the trailing 60 s and the union of distinct matches touched, against `cap = max(1, round(base × RS))` from `max_bets_per_minute` / `max_matches_per_minute` (defaults 12 / 10). Rejects as `rejected_velocity` (reason `velocity_bets_per_minute_exceeded` / `velocity_matches_per_minute_exceeded`), logged to `riskzilla_event_log` for USDC and surfaced in the betticker / bets pills. **Behaviour scoring** — pure functions in [`services/api/src/lib/riskzilla/behaviour-score.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/riskzilla/behaviour-score.ts) (unit-tested): per session, `straightness` (chord/path over 6-point windows; interpolated bot moves ≈ 1.0), `speedCv`, `headingEntropy`, `clicksWithoutApproach` (no pointer sample in the 1.5 s before a click — `page.click()` teleports), `clickIntervalCv`; each ramps to a suspicion in [0, 1], the weighted mean is the session score, and fewer than two usable components → NULL (touch devices produce no pointer data; they are left unscored, not flagged). [`behaviour-sweeper.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/riskzilla/behaviour-sweeper.ts) runs every 5 min under a Redis NX lock (`BEHAVIOUR_SWEEPER_DISABLED=1` skips): scores ≤200 settled signed-in `analytics_sessions` with new data, rolls each touched bettor up into `bettor_behaviour_scores` (session scores weighted by sample count over 30 days, blended 80/20 with the share of tickets confirmed within `min_human_ms + 150 ms`), and sets `alert` at `behaviour_alert_threshold` (default 0.7) over ≥ `behaviour_min_sessions` (default 2) with 0.1 hysteresis; a newly raised alert clears any earlier acknowledgement. **Not on the hot path**: placement only ever reads the precomputed row. Config loader [`bot-controls.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/riskzilla/bot-controls.ts) (5 s in-process memo; Postgres, never Redis — see 0095). Admin — [`admin/riskzilla/bot-controls.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/riskzilla/bot-controls.ts) (GET/PUT, audit-logged) + [`admin/riskzilla/behaviour.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/riskzilla/behaviour.ts) (`/admin/riskzilla/behaviour/alerts`, per-bettor `behaviour/acknowledge` + `behaviour/rescore`, audit-logged); `/admin/riskzilla/bettors` gains `botScore` / `botAlert` + a `bot_score` sort, the profile gains a `behaviour` block, the dashboard an `automationAlerts` count. UI — new **Bot controls** tab at [`apps/web/src/app/admin/riskzilla/bot-controls/`](./apps/web/src/app/admin/riskzilla/bot-controls/) (config editor + alerts table with Acknowledge / Re-open), a red alert banner + "Automation signals" panel with Rescore on the bettor profile ([`behaviour-panel.tsx`](./apps/web/src/app/admin/riskzilla/bettors/[id]/behaviour-panel.tsx)), a Bot column on the bettors list, and an Automation alerts KPI on the dashboard. Migrations `0096`–`0098`. |
| Catalog scraper friction (per-IP read limits + strict WS Origin) | Cheap layer on the public odds surface (2026-09-03), agreed to stop naive scripts only — anything a browser can fetch a script can fetch, so this is friction, not a wall. **Per-IP rate limits** on the odds-bearing catalog reads via per-route `config.rateLimit` (the api's limiter is `global: false`): `/catalog/sports/:slug`, `/catalog/matches`, `/catalog/matches/:id`, `/catalog/sports/:slug/tournaments`, and the per-match `zillatips` / `zillafacts` / `zillabuild` at 300/min; the polled `/catalog/matches/:id/boosted-odds` (5 s poll) and `/catalog/zillaboost-banners` (10 s) at 600/min; `/catalog/zillaflash` (2 s poll) at 900/min; `/catalog/search` keeps its earlier 60/min. Cheap Redis-cached, odds-free reads (`/catalog/sports`, `/catalog/live-counts`, `/catalog/combi-boost-config`) are deliberately unlimited — every (main) layout render fetches them. **Load-bearing detail:** SSR fetches come from the web replicas, so a per-IP limit would have throttled the whole storefront at ~40 page views/min site-wide. [`apps/web/src/lib/server-fetch.ts`](./apps/web/src/lib/server-fetch.ts) therefore forwards the visitor's IP as `X-Forwarded-For` (last inbound entry, shape-checked), the Caddyfile's `web_upstream` block now overwrites `X-Forwarded-For {remote_host}` like the api and ws blocks already did, and Fastify's `trustProxy: 1` takes that value as `request.ip` because the web container is the api's immediate peer. Limits are sized for an office or carrier NAT worth of tabs (a 2 s poll is 30/min per tab). **Strict WS Origin:** `docker-compose.yml` sets `CORS_ORIGINS_STRICT=${CORS_ORIGINS_STRICT:-true}` on ws-gateway, so an upgrade with no Origin header is refused (browsers always send one; nothing server-side opens `/ws`; the Android app is a WebView). Override to `false` in `.env` if a legitimate Origin-less client ever appears. |
| Per-bettor promo visibility (ZillaFlash + CombiBoost + ZillaBuild) | Engine — [`services/api/src/lib/bettor-promo-visibility.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/bettor-promo-visibility.ts) (`loadPromoVisibilityCascades` / `resolveVisible` — one query partitions all rows by `promo_kind`; resolver walks match > tournament > sport > global, returns `true` as the default when no row matches). Cascade rows in `bettor_promo_visibility_config` (migration 0071). Catalog: [`/catalog/zillaflash`](./services/api/src/modules/zillaflash/routes.ts) filters offers per match's cascade resolution when authed; [`/catalog/combi-boost-config`](./services/api/src/modules/catalog/routes.ts) returns `enabled=false` when the user's global combi_boost cascade resolves to false. Placement: [`/bets`](./services/api/src/modules/bets/routes.ts) re-resolves every ZillaFlash leg's (match, tournament, sport) and rejects with `zillaflash_offer_unavailable` if hidden; [`bets/service.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/bets/service.ts) strips combi-boost multiplier (sets to 1.0, omits `bet_meta.boostMultiplier`) if ANY leg resolves to hidden — defense in depth so stale clients can't claim a promo the bettor can't see. Admin routes at [`services/api/src/modules/admin/promo-visibility.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/promo-visibility.ts) (summary + tree with all three promo kinds per row + scope CRUD; every mutation audit-logged). Admin UI at [`apps/web/src/app/admin/users/[id]/promo-visibility/`](./apps/web/src/app/admin/users/[id]/promo-visibility/) — sport → tournament → match tree with three `On / Off` toggle columns per row (ZillaFlash / CombiBoost / ZillaBuild) + Clear button per kind to drop the override and inherit from parent scope. User-detail page picks up a summary card linking here. |
| ZillaBuild (pre-built BetBuilder cards) | Engine — [`services/api/src/modules/zillabuild/engine.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/zillabuild/engine.ts) (`getZillaBuildForMatch` — OBB `availableMarkets(eventUrn)` → reverse-map to internal markets via `(provider_market_id, canonical(specifiers))` → keep only map-scoped markets in the admin `provider_market_id` allowlist (empty = all) → per (map, slot) KEEP the persisted card if its legs still price, else GENERATE a random 2-4 leg single-map combo validated by OBB `sessionCreate` above the `min_combined_odds` floor → re-quote every read. Prematch-only. Composition persisted in `zillabuild_cards`; combined + per-leg odds are re-quoted from OBB and NEVER stored. Redis cache `zillabuild:v1:{matchId}` (config `cache_ttl_seconds`) + NX gen lock `zillabuild:gen:{matchId}`. Labels via the catalog's `renderOutcomeLabel` / `substituteTemplate` (en). Graceful-idle: OBB disabled/down → empty payload, section hides. Pure combinatorics unit-tested in [`engine.test.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/zillabuild/engine.test.ts)). Catalog route — [`/catalog/matches/:matchId/zillabuild`](./services/api/src/modules/zillabuild/routes.ts) applies the per-bettor `zillabuild` promo-visibility gate (display-only — a card just pre-loads the standard BetBuilder slip, so `/bets` is unchanged). Admin config — [`/admin/zillabuild-config`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/zillabuild-config.ts) (singleton `zillabuild_config`: enabled, eligible `provider_market_id` allowlist, cards-per-map, map-count, legs range, min combined odds, cache TTL; audit-logged). Admin UI — [`apps/web/src/app/admin/zillabuild/`](./apps/web/src/app/admin/zillabuild/) (`page.tsx` + `zillabuild-config-editor.tsx`); sidebar entry under **Storefront → ZillaBuild**. Storefront — hook [`apps/web/src/lib/use-zillabuild.ts`](./apps/web/src/lib/use-zillabuild.ts) + widget [`apps/web/src/components/match/zillabuild-cards.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/match/zillabuild-cards.tsx) mounted prematch-only on the match page between ZillaFacts and the markets tree; "Use this build" loads the combo into the slip's BetBuilder mode (`setBetbuilderMatch` + `add`). Shared types [`packages/types/src/zillabuild.ts`](./packages/types/src/zillabuild.ts). Migration `0082_zillabuild`. |
| Custom Boosted Odds / **ZillaBoost** (operator-facing name) | Banners — [`services/api/src/modules/boosted-odds/banners.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/boosted-odds/banners.ts) (`GET /catalog/zillaboost-banners`, migration 0086) + storefront [`apps/web/src/components/lobby/zillaboost-banners.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/lobby/zillaboost-banners.tsx) (home page: tournament banner / scoreless match card / ZillaFlash-style market card) + sidebar bolt via [`useZillaBoostSportSet`](./apps/web/src/lib/use-zillaboost-banners.ts). Admin board at [`apps/web/src/app/admin/boosted-odds/`](./apps/web/src/app/admin/boosted-odds/) is storefront-shaped (sports rail + match cards + tournament chips + Teams tab); the popup sets boost % / end (no end / duration presets / exact time) / Min RS / promo banner. **Selection scope** (migrations `0087`+`0088`): each market row in the expanded match card has an expand caret that lists the market's live selections with their current bettor-facing prices, each individually boostable — the delta comes out of that outcome's own implied probability so its siblings keep their raw price. Engine helpers — [`services/api/src/lib/boosted-odds.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/boosted-odds.ts) (`loadBoostRulesForMatch` / `resolveBoostForMarket` / `loadQuotableOutcomes` / `loadSelectionBoostedMarketIds` / `quoteBoostedMarket`, `passesRiskGate`, and the placement validator `validateCustomBoostForBet`). **All boost pricing goes through ONE shared function** — `quoteMarketBoost` in [`packages/types/src/netwinstable.ts`](./packages/types/src/netwinstable.ts) — called by the match page (realtime, per WS tick), the banners endpoint (server-side per poll), and `POST /bets` (placement re-validation). It owns the fair-book clamp, the per-selection probability-share clamp (`SELECTION_BOOST_MAX_PROB_SHARE`, half — the fair-book clamp alone can't bound a single cell, since a market's headroom can exceed a longshot's whole probability and send its price to infinity), the proportional sharing of scarce headroom across several boosted selections in one market, and the **replacement precedence**: a market carrying any eligible selection rule is priced by its selection rules ALONE (composing them with a market/match-wide key boost would push the book past fair). `formatBoostedOdds` + `isQuotableOutcomeOdds` live in the same module so `boosted-odds.ts` stays free of relative imports — apps/web imports it as a VALUE and the package is NodeNext, so a `.js`-suffixed relative import there passes `tsc` but fails `next build` (see the `oddzilla-types-barrel-imports` footgun). Catalog — [`GET /catalog/matches/:id/boosted-odds`](./services/api/src/modules/boosted-odds/routes.ts) serves RULES only, never prices: per-market entries for market-scope rules, a flat `selections` array for outcome-scope rules (those market ids are stable — the rule pins the row), plus ONE cascade-resolved `matchWide` object (match > competitor > tournament > sport) the client applies to whatever markets it currently renders. Market-scope entries are omitted for markets that have selection rules, and the banner endpoint skips those markets too, so no surface ever advertises a price placement would reject. **Deliberately no market enumeration** — live matches create new ladder-line market rows on odds updates and Oddin suspends the whole book between rounds; the first per-market-flattened version of this endpoint flashed every boost off whenever a poll landed in that window (fixed 2026-08-26). Anonymous-friendly; RS gate treats logged-out viewers as RS 1.000. Outcomes priced at exactly 1.00 stay in the boost computation everywhere (client / placement / banners in parity) so a live favorite ticking through 1.00 can't collapse its market's boost. Placement — [`bets/routes.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/bets/routes.ts) validates per-leg `boostedOddsRuleId` and overwrites `odds` with the authoritative boosted value (±0.01 tolerance, mirrors the ZillaFlash block; a leg carrying both ids keeps the flash offer); [`bets/service.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/bets/service.ts) treats it as the server price and stamps `ticket_selections.boost_rule_id`; the bet-delay worker ([`worker.go`](./services/bet-delay/internal/worker/worker.go)) skips per-leg drift for stamped legs. Settlement is untouched — payout reads `odds_at_placement`. Admin — [`services/api/src/modules/admin/boosted-odds.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/boosted-odds.ts) (rules overview + sport → tournament/team → match → market → **selection** tree endpoints + PUT/DELETE rule upsert, audit-logged; `GET /admin/boosted-odds/markets/:marketId/selections` backs the per-market expand row and returns inactive / unpriced outcomes flagged rather than hidden, since Oddin drops individual outcomes inactive for seconds at a time mid-round) and UI at [`apps/web/src/app/admin/boosted-odds/`](./apps/web/src/app/admin/boosted-odds/) (green Boost button per row, popup with boost % / end time / Min Risk Score); sidebar entry under **Risk & limits** below ZillaFlash. A market row shows a `N sel` chip when it has boosted selections, and its own market badge greys to `inactive` because the selections have taken over its pricing. **Team boosts (migration 0093):** a competitor rule now picks its market span via `competitor_markets` — `all` (every market of every match the team plays, including the opponent's side and symmetric markets; the original behaviour and the default, so no existing row changed) or `team_only` (just the team's OWN outcome, and only in team-shaped markets). A `team_only` rule is priced through the SELECTION path — the delta comes out of that outcome's own implied probability so the opponent's price never moves — which also means both teams in a match can carry their own team_only boost simultaneously, something the market-wide "higher pct wins" tie-break couldn't express. Team-shaped = `provider_market_id IN (1, 4)` (match winner / map winner, where outcome `1` is the home competitor and `2` the away one); the predicate is [`isTeamShapedMarket`](./packages/types/src/boosted-odds.ts), shared by server and browser rather than copied a third time (ZillaTips' team-of-interest map and the banner endpoint's `teamShaped` flag already keyed off the same two ids). Delivered to the client as `matchWide.teamOutcomeId` — an INSTRUCTION ("boost this outcome wherever the market is team-shaped"), never a market list, because a live match mints new market rows as maps start and an enumerated list would silently miss them. `BatchedMatchBoosts` was collapsed from a `marketWide()` / `selections()` pair into one `resolve(ctx, marketId, providerMarketId)` for the same reason: the split version let a caller take a competitor rule as market-wide by accident. Competitor scope also gains a **banner** (team card linking to `/sport/:slug?team=<id>`, no odds — the rule spans many matches, so there is no single price to quote; the copy says whether it boosts the team's own prices or the whole match). **Fair-odds warning:** the active-rules overview shows a red `!` when the requested boost can't be delivered, because `boostMarketKey` floors its target key at 1.0 and silently cuts the delta on a tight book — an operator would otherwise see a green "+13%" badge while the storefront delivered 7%. Computed exactly in SQL rather than by pricing every market, since the whole clamp reduces to the book key (`bookKey = Σ(1/odds)`): clamped ⇔ `key < 1 + pct/100`, dead ⇔ `key <= 1`, best deliverable pct on a clamped market = `(key − 1) × 100`. Outcome-scope rules are excluded — their binding limit is usually `SELECTION_BOOST_MAX_PROB_SHARE`, so the fair-book number alone would mislead. The algebra is pinned against the real `boostMarketKey` in [`boosted-odds.test.ts`](./packages/types/src/boosted-odds.test.ts) so the two can't drift. **Scheduling (migration 0092):** the popup's **Starts** field (Immediately / Schedule) sets `starts_at`, so a boost can be configured in advance and activate later — nothing renders, prices, or pays out before it. Scheduled rules show grey with `from <time>` in the overview and rail rather than claiming an active boost. One combination is refused client-side: scheduled-start + duration-end, because a duration is measured from SAVE time and would make the rule begin already-expired. Tournaments carry a `T<n>` risk-tier badge (`TierBadge`) in three places — the tournament chip strip, each match card's tournament column, and the active-rules overview — because the tier sets RiskZilla's per-tier match liability cap, so the same boost pct means very different exposure on a tier-1 final than a tier-9 qualifier; a tournament with no Oddin tier renders `T—` rather than omitting the badge, since "unset" is itself information and a missing badge is indistinguishable from a render bug. Storefront **list cards** (lobby / sport page / `/live` / `/upcoming`) price their inline match-winner row SERVER-side — a card has no per-outcome WS subscription, so `/catalog/sports/:slug` + `/catalog/matches` resolve the cascade via the batched [`loadBoostRulesForMatches`](./services/api/src/lib/boosted-odds.ts) (one query covers all six tiers for the whole page) and price it through the same `quoteMarketBoost`. The boost is computed from the RAW published odds and REPLACES the per-bettor adjusted price, mirroring placement exactly (`bets/service.ts` branches on `boostedOddsRuleId` before the adjustment branch, so a boosted leg is priced from raw and skips the adjustment); a suspended outcome stays null so a boost can never resurrect an unbettable cell. The payload also carries the RESOLVED rule (`boostRule` / `boostSelections`) so [`mergeMatchWithLive`](./apps/web/src/components/match/match-list-tabs.tsx) re-applies the boost over each WS tick instead of reverting to raw — without that the boost flickers off on the first live update (same bug 32286a7 fixed on the match page), and the no-tick fallback must use the pre-boost original or the boost compounds per tick. `MatchRow` carries `boost.ruleId` into the slip: without it placement falls back to the raw price and, since a typical boost sits INSIDE the 5% drift tolerance, the bet is silently accepted at the lower raw price rather than rejected. Storefront **match page** — **prices are realtime**: the match page computes boosted prices client-side through the shared `quoteMarketBoost` over the live outcome set it already tracks via WS ticks, so the boosted price re-derives in the same render as the tick that moved the raw odds; the hook [`apps/web/src/lib/use-boosted-odds.ts`](./apps/web/src/lib/use-boosted-odds.ts) polls only the RULES (5 s — admin config propagation + per-viewer Min-RS gate, which is per-user and thus can't ride the shared `odds:match:{id}` channel) and exposes `selectionsByMarket` alongside `byMarket` / `matchWide`, and [`live-markets.tsx`](./apps/web/src/app/(main)/match/[id]/live-markets.tsx) merges the computed entries with ZillaFlash into `AnyBoostEntry` (flash wins on overlap; custom chip shows a countdown ONLY when `ends_at` is set and within an hour). On a selection-boosted market only the boosted cells wear the green styling + struck original price; the header chip still renders once. Slip legs freeze WS price ticks while boosted (active flag still applies) in [`bet-slip-rail.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/shell/bet-slip-rail.tsx). Shared types [`packages/types/src/boosted-odds.ts`](./packages/types/src/boosted-odds.ts). Migrations `0085_boosted_odds`, `0086_zillaboost_banner`, `0087_boosted_odds_outcome_scope`, `0088_boosted_odds_outcome_rules`. |
| ZillaBoost graphics banners (AI-generated promo art) | Admin popup option **Generate graphics banner** (implies the promo banner; available for every scope that HAS a banner surface — so sport / tournament / competitor / match / market, but not `outcome`, which has none. Competitor became eligible with migration 0093 when team banners shipped; the job context already carried `competitorName` + brand colour, so the prompt path needed no change, and the team banner renders the art as a scrimmed backdrop like the sport and tournament ones) → `boosted_odds_config.graphics_banner` + one queue row in `zillaboost_banner_image_jobs` (migration `0089`; rule_id PK — regenerate resets the SAME row, previous image stays until the replacement lands so the storefront banner never blanks). **Pull model, worker on the OPERATOR PC** (same shape as `services/support-ai-bot` — this is deliberate and security-load-bearing): [`services/zillaboost-banner-gen`](./services/zillaboost-banner-gen/) runs on the machine hosting ComfyUI, polls [`/webhooks/banner-gen/:secret/*`](./services/api/src/modules/boosted-odds/banner-gen.ts) over outbound HTTPS (constant-time token = `BANNER_GEN_TOKEN`, graceful-idle 503 when unset), and talks to ComfyUI + LM Studio on `127.0.0.1`. **ComfyUI must never listen beyond loopback** — it is unauthenticated admin-level code execution (workflow graphs are programs; `--enable-manager` installs arbitrary custom nodes), so the server never gets a network path to it. A server-side compose variant reaching the PC over tailscale existed for a few hours on 2026-08-28 and was reverted the same day; tailscale was purged from the box. The worker probes ComfyUI before claiming and sleeps 1 h between probes while it's down (no attempts burned) — "PC off" = jobs wait in the queue and drain on boot. It POSTs `/heartbeat` every 30 s → Redis `bannergen:worker:online` (TTL 90 s) → the "Image worker online" dot + queue counts on `/admin/boosted-odds`. On the RX 7900 XTX box keep `IMAGE_MODEL=flux1-dev-fp8.safetensors` pinned: SD3.5-fp8 crashes its ROCm kernels (`hipErrorLaunchFailure`), and the FLUX path uses the box-verified graph (EmptyLatentImage, sampler cfg 1.0) with an explicit FluxGuidance node at 2.5 and 28 steps. Per job: Wikipedia research on the boosted entities → LM Studio authors a diffusion prompt (the prompt half accepts **any** OpenAI-compatible endpoint via `LM_STUDIO_BASE_URL` + `LM_STUDIO_API_KEY`, so it can move to a hosted gateway; ComfyUI stays local either way. `max_tokens` is 1200 rather than 400 because reasoning models spend the budget on an internal pass first — measured 969 reasoning + 212 text tokens on GLM-5.3-flash, so 400 would have returned an empty prompt) → **ComfyUI** render (POST `/prompt` with a fixed txt2img graph, poll `/history`, fetch `/view`; 1920×640 plate, ~1.2 MP — the original 1152×384 was under half the pixel budget these models train at, which is where mushy faces and duplicated subjects came from) → composite real crests + names (`compose.ts`, sharp) → downscale to 1536×512 → base64 upload (4 MB cap, png/jpeg/webp; WebP by default). **The game's appearance is ground truth supplied to the prompt, not asked for** — [`game-vocab.ts`](./services/zillaboost-banner-gen/src/game-vocab.ts) maps all 32 active sport slugs to TWO clauses — `scene` (what is in the frame) and `style` (how that title actually looks: a Source 2 frame, Valve painted key art, Riot splash art, a broadcast frame) — both appended deterministically so no completion can drop them. `scene` exists because the local LLM does NOT reliably know these titles and left to itself rendered a DOTA 2 match as tactical-shooter soldiers with melee weapons (2026-08-28); `style` exists because asking for "premium esports promo art" produced the same airbrushed picture for all 32 slugs (2026-08-28). The prompt is also PROSE, not tag soup — FLUX reads sentences (T5), and the SD1.5-era `cinematic, dramatic lighting, depth of field` shape only summons the machine-made look; [`prompt.ts`](./services/zillaboost-banner-gen/src/prompt.ts) keeps a banned-vocabulary list that is both forbidden in the system prompt AND stripped from the completion (`scrubSlop`), because a small local model agrees not to use those words and then uses them anyway. Team identity comes from `competitors.brand_color` / `tournaments.brand_color` carried on the job context: the prompt demands a VERSUS composition built from the two teams' actual hex colours. **Real crests and real team names are COMPOSITED onto the finished plate** ([`compose.ts`](./services/zillaboost-banner-gen/src/compose.ts), sharp) — diffusion at banner scale draws a crest as a smeared blob and a name as mangled pseudo-letters, the loudest AI tell there is, so the prompt still forbids both and the genuine assets go on top: crests fetched from `competitors.logo_url` (Oddin CDN or our own byte-serve, sent ABSOLUTE on the job context), names set in a real font over a bottom scrim with a VS mark. **The overlay is sized against the DISPLAY size, not the file**: the plate is 1536×512 and the card paints it into a ~411×137 strip, so the first cut's perfectly reasonable 25px type arrived as an illegible 6.7px — crests and names are now ~34px / ~13px on screen, which looks oversized when you open the PNG and correct where anyone sees it (the tournament line was dropped for the same reason: 4.3px, and the card prints it in crisp UI type just below). Match / market scope ONLY — sport and tournament plates stay bare because the storefront uses those as backdrops with its own copy laid over them, so baked copy would collide and duplicate. Every part is fail-soft: a crest that 404s is skipped, a font that will not rasterise drops the text layer, and sharp missing entirely (new code unpacked on the GPU box without `pnpm install`) ships the bare plate. Prompt + params are echoed back on upload and shown in the admin panel (migrations 0090/0091). Guardrails in the prompt: no text/numbers/logos/people in the RENDER, and the area the overlay lands on kept simple (bottom third on match/market plates, left third on sport/tournament plates). Claim lease 15 min (crash self-returns); real generation failures back off 1 h/attempt, `failed` after 24 (red chip in the admin overview; untick + re-tick to reset). The chip is a **toggle**: expanding it opens a troubleshooting panel with the rendered image beside the exact prompt (selectable + Copy button) and the render params — `seed` + `checkpoint` make a render reproducible by hand in ComfyUI. The worker also POSTs `/heartbeat` every 30 s on its own timer (fire-and-forget, so it keeps beating through multi-minute renders) → Redis `bannergen:worker:online` (TTL 90 s) → the "Image worker online/offline" dot + queue counts strip above the rules table; offline is a normal state, not an error. **Body limits are load-bearing**: the `/complete` route needs its own `bodyLimit` (8 MiB — the 4 MiB decoded cap is ~5.6 MiB as base64) and Caddy needs the matching `@banner_gen_uploads` carve-out above its 1 MiB default, or uploads 413 AFTER a successful render (hit on the first live one). The worker lives on the GPU box (`DESKTOP-IO524Q2`, `ssh localserver`) at `D:\AI\Oddzilla`, run by the `ZillaboostWorker` scheduled task — **updating it is not `git pull`** (that box has no usable git credentials; use the git-bundle procedure in [`docs/OPERATIONS.md`](./docs/OPERATIONS.md), and confirm the restart took new code via the Redis heartbeat key, not the task state). Verified end-to-end 2026-08-28: a CS2 match regenerated with a true versus composition (crimson left / blue right over bomb-site concrete and smoke), prompt + params stored. On the RX 7900 XTX box `IMAGE_MODEL` must stay pinned to FLUX — `sd3.5_large_fp8_scaled` crashes its ROCm kernels with `hipErrorLaunchFailure` and leaves ComfyUI's GPU context wedged (needs a process restart), while the FLUX path uses the box-verified graph (`EmptyLatentImage`, sampler cfg 1.0, `FluxGuidance` 2.5, 28 steps). Image BYTES live on the job row — NOT on `boosted_odds_config`, whose full-row selects ride hot catalog paths. Byte-serve `GET /catalog/zillaboost-banners/:ruleId/image` (immutable + `?v=<generated-ms>`); the banners payload carries `imageUrl` per scope entry and the storefront renders it as a scrimmed backdrop (sport/tournament strips, light-on-dark content) or a 3:1 top strip (match/market cards), with onError falling back to the plain banner. **The 3:1 strip full-bleeds by cancelling the card's 10px/12px padding with negative margins, and that needs `maxWidth: "none"` on the img** — Tailwind preflight's `img, video { max-width: 100% }` in `@layer base` otherwise clamps the `calc(100% + 24px)` back to 100%, which does not centre the image but dumps the whole 24px on the RIGHT edge as a white gap (measured on production 2026-08-28: 328px card, 302px image, 25px gap). Any future full-bleed image needs the same opt-out. |
| Per-bettor odds adjustment (engine + admin) | Engine — [`services/api/src/lib/bettor-odds-adjustment.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/bettor-odds-adjustment.ts) (`loadBettorAdjustmentCascade` / `resolveBettorAdjustmentBp` / `applyBettorAdjustment` — multiplies raw odds by `(1 + bp/10000)`, clamps to `[ADJUSTED_ODDS_FLOOR=1.001, 1/probability]` (fair-odds ceiling plus a low floor so a negative bp on near-1.0 raw odds can't dip the bettor-facing price below 1.0), returns the same up-to-4dp trimmed-to-≥2dp string formatOdds produces). Mirror Go port in [`services/bet-delay/internal/worker/adjust.go`](./services/bet-delay/internal/worker/adjust.go) so the worker's drift check evaluates against the same adjusted price the slip captured. Cascade rows in `bettor_odds_adjustment_config` (migration 0070). Applied per request inside [`services/api/src/modules/catalog/routes.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/catalog/routes.ts) (sport list / cross-sport list / match-detail / Top market helper), inside the placement tx in [`services/api/src/modules/bets/service.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/bets/service.ts), and inside `evaluate()` in [`services/bet-delay/internal/worker/worker.go`](./services/bet-delay/internal/worker/worker.go). Live WS-side application — [`services/ws-gateway/src/bettor-adjustment.ts`](./services/ws-gateway/src/bettor-adjustment.ts) holds the per-user cascade cache; `dispatchOdds` in [`services/ws-gateway/src/server.ts`](./services/ws-gateway/src/server.ts) applies bp per subscriber with a zero-allocation fast path (bp=0) and a per-bp memoisation across subscribers of the same match. Payload extension to carry `sportId` + `tournamentId` lives in [`services/odds-publisher/internal/publisher/publisher.go`](./services/odds-publisher/internal/publisher/publisher.go) (`OutboundPayload`). Invalidation channel `bettor_adjustment_invalidated` — published by admin routes at [`services/api/src/modules/admin/odds-adjustment.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/odds-adjustment.ts) on every mutation (summary + sports / tournaments / matches tree + scope CRUD; every mutation audit-logged). Admin UI at [`apps/web/src/app/admin/users/[id]/odds-adjustment/`](./apps/web/src/app/admin/users/[id]/odds-adjustment/) (`page.tsx` + `tree-client.tsx` — sport → tournament → match tree with −1% / −0.5% / +0.5% / +1% stepper buttons; user-detail page picks up a summary card linking here). |
| FE analytics (first-party) | Intake — [`services/api/src/modules/analytics/routes.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/analytics/routes.ts) (`POST /analytics/collect`, anonymous-friendly — the auth plugin's cookie preHandler supplies `request.user` when present; 120/min rate limit, 256 KiB body cap, zod caps from `packages/types/src/analytics.ts`, timestamps clamped to a sane window; session upsert never re-owns a session (`COALESCE(user_id, EXCLUDED.user_id)`), and the denormalised counters are bumped by rows that actually inserted so re-delivered batches are no-ops. Also exports `startAnalyticsRetentionSweeper` — hourly Redis NX-lock sweep, 90 d sessions/events + 14 d mouse batches, `ANALYTICS_SWEEPER_DISABLED=1` to skip). Admin read API — [`services/api/src/modules/admin/analytics.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/analytics.ts) (overview KPIs incl. avg/median session length, sessions/day, popular sections/paths, top clicked elements by precomputed `payload->>'label'`; keyset-paginated sessions list with email/uuid search; per-session journey (events by seq) + lazy mouse-trail fetch per path). Tracker — [`apps/web/src/lib/analytics/tracker.ts`](./apps/web/src/lib/analytics/tracker.ts) (per-tab session in `sessionStorage["oz:analytics:session"]` renewed after 30 min idle; page views on every route change, every click with a compact target descriptor — annotate elements with `data-oz-track` for stable labels; mouse sampling one point / 120 ms while moving; 30 s visibility heartbeats; 5 s fetch flush + sendBeacon/keepalive final flush on pagehide/tab-hide; skips `navigator.webdriver` and `/admin` paths) mounted from the ROOT layout via [`analytics-tracker.tsx`](./apps/web/src/lib/analytics/analytics-tracker.tsx). Admin UI — [`apps/web/src/app/admin/analytics/`](./apps/web/src/app/admin/analytics/) (overview + `sessions/[id]` journey timeline + SVG mouse-trail replay with click rings); sidebar entry under **Operations → Analytics**. |
| Unsettled monitor (admin) | API [`services/api/src/modules/admin/unsettled.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/unsettled.ts) — `GET /admin/unsettled/{summary,matches,matches/:id/markets,tickets}`; UI [`apps/web/src/app/admin/unsettled/`](./apps/web/src/app/admin/unsettled/) (`page.tsx` + `tabs.tsx`), sidebar entry under **Operations** next to Wedged matches. **"Unsettled" = the match is over but the book never went terminal**: `matches.status IN ('closed','cancelled')` while `markets.status NOT IN (-3,-4)`. Only two things ever write a terminal market status — Oddin's `bet_settlement` (services/settlement) and Fonbet's results grader (`fonbet-ingester internal/settle`, gated by `FONBET_SETTLE_ENABLED`) — so a market neither reached stays open forever and any ticket on it stays `accepted` with the stake debited. Exists because the operator requirement is NO manual settlement, and the prerequisite for that is seeing everything that would currently need one. **Markets are grouped by match, not listed flat** (33 597 market rows across 580 matches when this shipped): the match is the unit an operator acts on, and the per-match drill-down carries the ladder-line rows. The tickets tab separates two cases a status column conflates — a leg waiting on a market that never settled, versus `allLegsResolved` where every leg has a result but the ticket was never paid, which is the stranded case settlement's own `ReconcileStranded` sweep heals. **Read-only by design**: settling / voiding / cancelling are money-moving decisions that belong behind their own audited endpoints, not behind a monitoring list. No sidebar badge — with tens of thousands of legacy rows it would be permanently red and stop meaning anything. |

uniform 7-day retention since `received_at`, with a periodic backfill that re-resolves `match_id` for rows whose URN now maps — closes the insert/auto-map race that previously left orphan rows hidden), `0012_reactivate_non_bot_sports` (re-enables every non-bot sport after the product opened past the 4-MVP scope), `0013_tournament_risk_tier`, `0014_multi_currency` (composite PK on `wallets`; `currency CHAR(4)` on `wallet_ledger` + `tickets`; OZ demo currency), `0015_cashout` (cashout cascade + probability columns + `cashouts` table), `0016_cashout_acceptance_delay`, `0017_tiple_tippot` (tiple/tippot bet products + per-scope `bet_product_config`), `0018_bet_product_per_leg_margin` (`bet_product_config.margin_bp_per_leg`; effective margin compounds multiplicatively as `(1 + margin_bp) × (1 + margin_bp_per_leg)^N − 1` — same shape a combo's overround takes via its odds product, so Tippot's all-wins multiplier stays below an equivalent combo), `0019_fe_market_display_order` (per-sport storefront ordering of market types — `(sport_id, provider_market_id) → display_order`;
