CHECK constraints mirror the API zod cap on bio length (≤280) and the `[A-Za-z0-9_]{3,20}` nickname format. `is_ai` is an internal flag for AI seed accounts in Phase 10.4 and is never serialised by any API endpoint per Decision D2.), `0025_community_tickets` (Phase 10.2 — `community_tickets` denormalised read-projection of publicly-resolved tickets driving the `/community` feed. `UNIQUE (ticket_id)` + `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` makes the upsert idempotent under settlement replay; rollback / re-settle generations update the same row. `sport_ids INTEGER[]` (GIN-indexed) carries the per-ticket sport set computed by joining `ticket_selections → markets → matches → tournaments → categories`. Verifier function `admin_audit_chain_check()` returns `(id, ok)` per row — any `ok=false` means the row was tampered with after insert. Migration backfills existing rows so the chain is valid from row 1.), `0027_deposit_reorg_safety` (security audit PR #130 — `deposits.block_hash TEXT` captured at insert time so the wallet-watcher processor can verify the canonical chain still contains the same block before crediting; reorg drops the block → row flips to `status='orphaned'` instead of crediting. Bytes are served by `GET /sports/:slug/logo` with a long immutable cache header; the upload endpoint stamps `sports.logo_url='/api/sports/<slug>/logo?v=<unix-ms>'` so the storefront's existing `<img src>` path picks the upload up and a re-upload busts the browser cache via the `?v` param. PATCH that overwrites `logo_url` with anything other than the byte-serve URL also clears `logo_data`/`logo_mime` in the same tx so bytes can't go orphan.), `0040_competitor_logo_upload` (mirror of 0039 for `competitors`: adds `logo_data BYTEA` + `logo_mime TEXT` paired by CHECK; same MIME allowlist; byte-serve at `GET /competitors/:id/logo` keyed by id since competitor slugs are scoped to (sportId, slug). New admin module at [`services/api/src/modules/admin/tournaments.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/tournaments.ts) (list / patch / upload / delete + audit-logged), public byte-serve `GET /tournaments/:id/logo` keyed by id since tournament slugs aren't globally unique.), `0045_user_devices` (push-notification device registry — `user_devices` table keyed by `(user_id, token)` with `revoked_at` soft-delete, partial index on active rows, and a CHECK enforcing the `platform IN (android, ios, web)` allowlist. Cross-user re-register revokes the prior owner so a hand-me-down phone doesn't keep pushing to the previous account. Server endpoints at `/devices/register`, `/devices/unregister`, `/devices` ([`devices/routes.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/devices/routes.ts)). The push-sender on the API is live in migration `0058_push_notifications_outbox` — durable outbox + LISTEN/NOTIFY worker dispatching via Firebase Admin SDK `sendEachForMulticast()`. Graceful-idle when the service-account JSON isn't mounted: rows still drain but with `last_error='firebase_disabled'`.), `0047_zillatips_state` (per-market historical ROI widget — `matches.live_started_at TIMESTAMPTZ` records the first not_started→live transition; backfilled from `scheduled_at` for already-live/closed matches so previously-played fixtures still anchor the lookback. `market_outcomes.prematch_odds NUMERIC(10,4)` is the published-odds snapshot captured at that transition (90-day `odds_history` retention forced this — the snapshot is the permanent record). **Both columns are backfilled inside the migration**: `live_started_at` from `scheduled_at`, and `prematch_odds` from the latest `odds_history.published_odds` with `ts < live_started_at` for every match still inside the 90-day partition window (CROSS JOIN LATERAL with `LIMIT 1`, gated `AND published_odds IS NOT NULL` so suspended ticks don't poison the snapshot). Matches whose odds_history partitions have been pruned (>90 days old) stay NULL and degrade gracefully — the ROI calc excludes unrated legs from its denominator. Capture path going forward is feed-ingester's `UpdateMatchStatus`: when the SQL guard observes the first transition to `live`, a single CTE update both stamps `live_started_at` and copies `published_odds → prematch_odds` for every market_outcome on the match (IS NULL guards keep it idempotent under recovery replay). Lookup indexes `matches_home_team_recency_idx` / `matches_away_team_recency_idx` are partial composites over (competitor_id, status, live_started_at DESC) WHERE live_started_at IS NOT NULL — index-scan top-5 per team without a sort step.), `0048_wrong_token_deposits` (admin visibility for users who send the wrong coin to the USDC receive address — `deposit_intents` picks up `detected_token_contract` + `detected_token_amount_raw NUMERIC(78,0)` + `acknowledged_at` / `acknowledged_by_user_id`, with a partial index on the unacked-wrong-token slice so the sidebar badge count is a tiny scan. New table `unattributed_deposits` records ANY ERC20 Transfer to the receive address whose contract isn't USDC — filled by a second `eth_getLogs` scan in wallet-watcher's `DiscoverIncoming` that drops the contract filter (`GetAllTransfersTo`), unique on `(network, tx_hash, log_index)` so multi-Transfer txs don't collide. The intent path also covers the "user pasted hash" case: `Inspect()` does a second pass for any Transfer to the receive address from a non-USDC contract and routes through `RejectIntentWrongToken` which stamps the diagnostic columns and opportunistically acks any matching `unattributed_deposits` row so the same incident doesn't double-alert. Token symbol + decimals are best-effort `eth_call` enrichment cached per contract for the service lifetime; unknown values are persisted as NULL and the UI renders the raw uint256 amount.), `0049_bank_ledger_types` + `0050_bank_real_balance` (bank now reflects the operator's REAL crypto cash — `seed + Σ credited deposits − Σ confirmed withdrawals + admin manual adjustments`. Bet outcomes (bet_loss/bet_payout/bet_refund) NO LONGER move `bank_limit_micro` — they only redistribute between bettor wallets and operator profit. 0049 adds `deposit_credit` + `withdrawal_debit` enum values; 0050 backfills historical `deposit_intents WHERE status='credited'` + legacy `deposits WHERE status='credited'` + `withdrawals WHERE status='confirmed'` as ledger rows, writes a synthetic `seed` baseline row equal to `current_bank_limit_micro − Σ(existing ledger deltas)` (the implicit column default of 100K USDC that was never written as a row), then recomputes `bank_limit_micro = current − Σ(bet_loss+bet_payout+bet_refund) − Σ(manual_adjust ref_type='ticket') + Σ(new deposit_credit) + Σ(new withdrawal_debit)`. Going forward: wallet-watcher `CreditIntent` and admin `/admin/deposits/:id/credit-manual` write `deposit_credit` rows + bump bank; admin `/admin/withdrawals/:id/mark-confirmed` writes `withdrawal_debit` row + decrements bank by `amount` only (fee stays as operator profit). Withdrawal-confirm now fails closed if the bank doesn't physically have the funds — the column CHECK `bank_limit_micro >= 0` rolls back the tx, surfacing reconciliation drift.), `0051_map_round_history` (per-(match, map) round-by-round capture powering ZillaFacts' in-progress conditional patterns — "after winning the first 2 rounds, team won the map in X of last Y starts". Schema: `(match_id, map_number)` composite PK with `round_winners TEXT` (compact 'H'/'A' chronological string), `home_won_total` / `away_won_total` totals, CHECK constraints binding length to totals and locking the char allowlist. Feed-ingester's `handleOddsChange` upserts on every `odds_change` carrying a period with `homeWonRounds` / `awayWonRounds` — forward-only delta append via `repeat()` in a single SQL statement. No backfill; round-prefix patterns light up as new matches play after deploy. Used by `services/api/src/modules/zillafacts/routes.ts` (`ROUND_PREFIX_PATTERNS` catalog) — per-map aggregation with rate-shape "80% over 5+ qualifying maps" surface threshold instead of streak-shape "5 in a row from newest".), `0052_riskzilla_live_bet_delay` (live bet acceptance delay cascade — new `live_delay_scope` enum (`global` / `sport` / `tournament` / `match`) and `riskzilla_live_delay_config` table. One typed FK per scope tier (`sport_id` / `tournament_id` / `match_id`) so ON DELETE CASCADE works for free; partial unique index per scope guarantees at most one row per (scope, ref). Migration seeds the global row at 5s. At placement, [`services/api/src/modules/bets/service.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/bets/service.ts) loads every relevant config row in ONE query (global + sport IN ... + tournament IN ... + match IN ...), resolves each live leg via `match > tournament > sport > global` (first non-NULL wins), takes MAX across live legs, then takes MAX with `users.bet_delay_seconds`. **Pure-prematch placements get ZERO delay** — the whole delay computation (per-user knob + cascade) is gated on at least one live leg being present; `users.bet_delay_seconds` only takes effect when a live leg is on the ticket. Admin UI at `/admin/riskzilla/live-delay` is a lazy-loaded tree; the global row cannot be deleted (it's the floor of the cascade). The bet-delay worker is unchanged — `ticket.not_before_ts` carries the agreed window across both sources.), `0053_accept_odds_changes` (bettor opt-in for the live-bet acceptance delay — adds `tickets.accept_odds_changes BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE`. When set, the bet-delay worker re-prices the ticket at the latest published odds inside its decision transaction instead of rejecting with `odds_drift_exceeded`: it overwrites each `ticket_selections.odds_at_placement`, recomputes `tickets.potential_payout_micro` from the new product odds (preserving the `bet_meta.boostMultiplier` frozen at placement), and bumps `riskzilla_bank_state.open_liability_micro` by the delta so the cached counter stays in sync until settlement decrements at the new value (**USDC-only** — see the RiskZilla engine row; an OZ re-price must not touch the counter at all). Single + combo only — the API service silently no-ops the flag for tiple / tippot / betbuilder (those products anchor pricing on probabilities / OBB session id at placement, not the per-leg published price). Suspended / inactive checks still reject regardless of the flag.), `0057_fe_market_order_per_map` (per-map-number ordering on the match-detail page — until this migration the `map` scope in `fe_market_display_order` was a single shared list that applied to every Map N tab. Each Map tab is now independently configurable. The CHECK constraint on `scope` is relaxed to accept `match`, `top`, or `map_<N>` where N is a positive integer; existing `map` rows are fanned out to `map_1..map_5` (BO5 is the deepest format the supported sports play, matching the storefront's `deriveScope()` id encoding) and then deleted. Catalog match-detail endpoint now looks up the ordering for each scope group via `Map<string, …>.get(group.id)` directly — no `match`/`map`/`top` enum, no fallback ladder. Admin UI at `/admin/fe-settings/markets-order` shows a dynamic chip strip per sport (Match / Map 1..N / Top), with the Map count discovered from the sport's markets (floor 5). The `top` scope keeps its existing curated-list behaviour; the storefront's `All` tab is unaffected (it just aggregates groups in native order and is intentionally not configurable).), `0058_push_notifications_outbox` (FCM push pipeline for winning bets — `push_notifications_outbox` durable table keyed by `(kind, ticket_id)` unique partial index for apply-once-on-settlement-replay. Producer is `services/settlement` Go: `maybeSettleTicket` writes a `bet_won` row inside the same tx as `SettleTicket` whenever `payout > stake_micro` (covers full wins + half-wins where the wallet went up); after the chunk tx commits, settlement fires `NOTIFY push_outbox` so the api worker drains immediately. Consumer is `services/api` TS: [`startPushOutboxWorker`](./services/api/src/modules/push/worker.ts) LISTENs on the channel + sweeps every 30 s with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, looks up active `user_devices`, calls Firebase Admin SDK `sendEachForMulticast`, soft-revokes dead FCM tokens (`messaging/invalid-argument` etc.), bumps `attempts` / `last_error` on transient failures up to `MAX_ATTEMPTS=5` then marks sent (give up). Graceful-idle: when `FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_PATH` is unset OR the JSON file is missing, the worker still runs but marks rows with `last_error='firebase_disabled'` so the queue drains and the table size stays bounded. Activation is operator-side only — generate a Firebase service-account JSON, drop at `/srv/oddzilla-firebase/service-account.json`, `make recreate api`.), `0060_zillapass` (quest / battle-pass scaffold — three tables. `zillapass_tasks` is the admin-curated catalog: stable `slug`, `title` / `description`, `target_count`, `predicate_key` (open-ended TEXT so new task kinds don't need a migration), `period` enum (daily / weekly / season), nullable `reward_kind` + `reward_payload jsonb`, `active`, `sort_order`. `zillapass_user_progress` is per-user, per-period progress with composite PK `(user_id, task_id, period_start)` so a daily task with the same slug has one row per day. `zillapass_user_state` is one row per user carrying `level` / `xp` / `active_streak_days` / `last_active_date` (lazily created on first read). Predicate-hook layer wired in `0061_zillapass_set_1`.), `0061_zillapass_set_1` (day-1 task set + set-shaped progress support — adds `progress_state JSONB` to `zillapass_user_progress` so predicates can dedupe (`{ sports: [...], matches: [...] }`) and seeds three tasks (idempotent ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING on slug): `profile-complete` (season; completes when both `users.nickname` and `users.avatar_template_id` are set; writer fires from PATCH /community/me/profile + PUT /community/me/avatar), `open-5-sports` (daily; predicate `sports_viewed` tracks distinct sport slugs from /sport/:slug mounts), `open-5-matches-different-sports` (daily; predicate `matches_viewed_diff_sports` tracks distinct sports across opened matches from /match/:id mounts, dedups repeat matches). Writers in [`services/api/src/modules/zillapass/writer.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/zillapass/writer.ts) use `SELECT FOR UPDATE` per (user, task, period_start) so concurrent nudges merge cleanly. Frontend trackers [`SportViewTracker` / `MatchViewTracker`](./apps/web/src/lib/zillapass-track.tsx) POST to `/zillapass/track` once per session per key (in-memory dedup) so repeat navs don't burn roundtrips.), `0062_zillapass_set_2` (second day-set, all counter-only predicates — currency-agnostic per product direction (a USDC bet and an OZ bet count the same)): `place-5-prematch-bets` ("Place 5 prematch singles" — daily, predicate `bets_prematch`; fires from POST /bets ONLY for single bets on a `not_started` match), `place-5-live-bets` ("Place 5 live singles" — daily, predicate `bets_live`; fires from POST /bets ONLY for single bets on a `live` match — mutually exclusive with `bets_prematch` so one single-bet placement bumps exactly one task. **Combos / tiples / tippots / betbuilder never count** — the writer gates on `legCount === 1`, which is equivalent to `betType='single'` since every other product enforces min-2-legs at placement time.), `change-market-tab-10` (daily, predicate `market_tab_changes`; counts user-driven scope-tab clicks on the match page, dedupes re-clicks on the active tab client-side and clamps at target_count server-side via `LEAST()`). Writer `incrementByPredicate` is a single ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE with `LEAST(current_count + delta, target_count)` so concurrent placements / clicks merge atomically without needing FOR UPDATE.), `0063_zillapass_task_wording` (idempotent UPDATE-by-slug to polish titles + descriptions across the six tasks shipped in 0061 + 0062. Re-running it is a no-op — the original INSERTs in 0061 + 0062 were updated in place so fresh installs ship with the new wording directly; this migration brings already-applied rows up to the same final state.), `0064_zillapass_stages` (per-user stage progression — adds `set_number INTEGER` to `zillapass_tasks` (backfilled: day-1 tasks ⇒ set 1, day-2 tasks ⇒ set 2; CHECK >= 1) and `current_set_number INTEGER DEFAULT 1` + `last_set_completed_date DATE` to `zillapass_user_state`. New index `zillapass_tasks_set_active_sort_idx` over `(set_number, active, sort_order, id)` for the per-set list lookup; drops the prior `(active, sort_order, id)` index since the new one is a strict superset. Tasks are now organised in ordered SETS; a user only sees tasks where `task.set_number = state.current_set_number`. After the user completes every active task in their current set on day X, the writer stamps `last_set_completed_date = X`. On the next /zillapass/me read where today (UTC) > stamp, the reader advances `current_set_number += 1` and clears the stamp. Different users sit on different stages — slow users stay on day-1 until they finish; fast users advance one set per UTC day at most.), `0065_user_role_namespace` (same email can back a bettor row AND an admin row — replaces the global `UNIQUE(email)` constraint with two partial unique indexes (`users_email_bettor_uniq WHERE role = 'user'`, `users_email_admin_uniq WHERE role IN ('admin','support')`) and strips the legacy `+admin@` suffix from every existing admin/support email in the same tx (cast to text to satisfy citext's regex limitations). `users.role` is the namespace discriminator — no new column. The auth layer reads the request host on every `/auth/login` and filters by the matching namespace via [`accountNamespaceFromRequest`](./services/api/src/lib/account-namespace.ts) (`oddzilla.cc` → bettor, `sadmin.oddzilla.cc` → admin), so the subdomain implicitly picks which account to authenticate. Public signup is hard-gated to the bettor namespace (admin host signup returns 401 `signup_disabled_here`). Pre-flight check confirmed zero in-admin-namespace collisions on the rename; the bare-email bettor accounts already existed for each renamed admin and coexist cleanly under the new partial indexes.), `0068_zillapass_set_3` (third stage — three daily sport-specific bet tasks: `place-5-bets-cs2` / `place-5-bets-dota2` / `place-5-bets-lol`, predicate keys `bets_sport_<slug>`. A ticket counts only when EVERY leg's match sits under the targeted sport — singles trivially qualify, pure-sport multibets count once, mixed-sport multibets fire no sport task. set_number = 3 with sport-page CTAs.), `0069_zillapass_set_4` (fourth stage — three daily product-specific bet tasks: `place-5-combos` / `place-5-tiples` / `place-5-tippots`, predicate keys `bets_product_<kind>` keyed off `tickets.bet_type`. BetBuilder is intentionally NOT a fourth task — it'd duplicate the all-legs-same-sport semantics for the special case of all-legs-same-match. set_number = 4 with `/upcoming` CTAs because combo/tiple/tippot is a slip-mode choice, not a dedicated page.), `0070_bettor_odds_adjustment` (per-bettor odds adjustment with cascade overrides — `bettor_odds_adjustment_config` table keyed by `(user_id, scope ∈ {global,sport,tournament,match}, ref-id)` with a signed `adjustment_bp INTEGER` in `[-9000, 9000]`. One typed FK column per scope tier (sport_id / tournament_id / match_id) so ON DELETE CASCADE works for free; partial unique indexes per (scope, ref) pin "at most one row per (user, scope, ref)" as a DB invariant. Cascade at catalog read / placement: `match > tournament > sport > global` (first non-NULL wins). Math: `adjusted = published × (1 + bp/10000)`, clamped to `[1.001, 1/probability]` — the high clamp is the operator-asked "can't go below zero margin / fair odds" ceiling (skipped silently when an outcome has no probability), and the low floor keeps the displayed price above 1.0 so a negative bp on near-1.0 raw odds can't produce sub-stake payouts. Applied at `/catalog/sports/:slug`, `/catalog/matches`, `/catalog/matches/:id` for the per-bettor SSR view; at `/bets` placement and the bet-delay worker for drift validation. Tiple / tippot / betbuilder skip the cascade — those products anchor on probability-based or OBB-session pricing, not per-leg published odds. Admin UI at `/admin/users/[id]/odds-adjustment` is the live-delay-style tree (global → sport → tournament → match) with −1% / −0.5% / +0.5% / +1% stepper buttons; the user-detail page picks up a summary card. ws-gateway also applies the cascade per-subscriber on every live odds tick (in-memory cascade cache keyed by userId, invalidated via Redis pub/sub channel `bettor_adjustment_invalidated` on every admin mutation) so the slip / live-odds display / placement all see the same adjusted price.), `0071_bettor_promo_visibility` (per-bettor cascade visibility for ZillaFlash + CombiBoost — unified `bettor_promo_visibility_config` table keyed by `(user_id, promo_kind ∈ {zillaflash, combi_boost}, scope, ref-id)` with `visible BOOLEAN`. One typed FK per scope tier and partial unique indexes per (user_id, promo_kind, scope, ref-id) so the cascade is a single index probe per tier. Default `visible=true` everywhere — opt-out model. Resolved at catalog read / placement: `match > tournament > sport > global` per (user, kind). Catalog filters `/catalog/zillaflash` per-offer + drops the CombiBoost multiplier strip from `/catalog/combi-boost-config` when the user's global combi_boost is hidden. Placement is the defense-in-depth gate: ZillaFlash legs the bettor can't see reject with `zillaflash_offer_unavailable`; CombiBoost multiplier is silently stripped (combiMultiplier=1.0, bet_meta.boostMultiplier omitted) when ANY leg's match / tournament / sport resolves to hidden. Admin tree at `/admin/users/[id]/promo-visibility` shows both kinds as two On/Off toggle columns per row.), `0072_user_hidden_sports` (per-bettor "hide this sport" preference — companion to `0056_user_sport_order`. Adds `users.hidden_sports TEXT[]` with the same 100-element CHECK cap. NULL or empty = nothing hidden. Slugs in the array are dropped from every storefront surface the bettor sees: the sidebar's live sport list, the lobby's match list + chip strip, `/upcoming`, `/live`, `/catalog/zillaflash` offers, and (transitively) the CombiBoost three-fold lobby carousel since its builder consumes the same already-filtered upcoming list. Sidebar edit mode keeps hidden sports rendered under a `Hidden` sub-header so the bettor can un-hide them. API: PUT `/users/me/hidden-sports` mirrors `/users/me/sport-order`. The /auth/me + /users/me payloads gain `hiddenSports`. Apply path is client-driven via `orderSportsForSidebar(sports, order, hidden)` + `orderMatchesBySport(matches, hidden)` in [`apps/web/src/lib/sport-order.ts`](./apps/web/src/lib/sport-order.ts); ZillaFlash filters server-side inside [`/catalog/zillaflash`](./services/api/src/modules/zillaflash/routes.ts) because the route is polled client-side and already user-aware. Slug format validated server-side via zod; the slug column is intentionally not FK-constrained so a sport rename doesn't invalidate saved preferences row-side.), `0073_zillapass_no_daily_reset` (operator decision: ZillaPass task progress no longer resets at the start of the next UTC day. Every existing `daily` / `weekly` task is migrated to `season`, which makes the writer / reader `periodStart` helper return the fixed `'2026-01-01'` anchor key for those tasks — every read + write lands on the same row regardless of calendar day. Per-day rows are collapsed into one row per (user_id, task_id) under the season anchor carrying `MAX(current_count)`, the `progress_state` from that same max-count row (matters for `sports_viewed` / `matches_viewed_diff_sports` whose state is a slug array), and the earliest `completed_at`. Conservative — we don't sum counters across days or UNION slug sets across days: a user who placed 3 bets one day and 2 another carries forward 3/5, not 5/5. Per-day rows are then deleted. Task descriptions seeded by 0061 / 0062 / 0068 / 0069 are rewritten to drop the now-inaccurate "today" / "this week" wording, matched by slug so an operator-customised description stays put. Column default for `period` flips from `'daily'` to `'season'` so admin-created tasks land in the no-reset bucket by default; admins can still explicitly pick `'daily'` / `'weekly'` via the editor — the writer's `periodStart` helper still honours those periods if they do.), `0073_email` (transactional email pipeline — adds `users.email_verified_at TIMESTAMPTZ NULL` (NULL = unverified; storefront shows a dismissible banner until the user clicks the link), `email_outbox` (durable queue mirroring `push_notifications_outbox`: enqueue from any route handler, drain via LISTEN/NOTIFY + 30 s sweep, apply-once via unique partial index on `(kind, dedup_key) WHERE dedup_key IS NOT NULL`), `email_verification_tokens` + `password_reset_tokens` (single-use 256-bit random tokens, hash-only storage — sha256(raw) lives in the DB, raw value lives only in the user's inbox; 24 h TTL for verify, 30 min for reset; consumption stamps `used_at` + burns every other unused token for the same user so a leaked older link can't be replayed). Signup wires `enqueueVerifyEmail` into the same Drizzle tx as the user-insert + wallet-create, so the email never goes out unless the user row commits. Forgot-password always returns 200 to prevent enumeration; reset-password hashes the new password and revokes every session for the user (cookie clear on the response so the current tab renders the logged-out shell immediately). Worker is graceful-idle when `EMAIL_PROVIDER_TOKEN` is unset — it still drains rows but stamps each `last_error='email_disabled'`, same shape FCM uses without a service-account JSON.), `0074_email_inbound` (backoffice inbox + outbound threading — adds `email_threads` (one row per conversation, denormalised counts + last_*_at maintained by the webhook handler / outbox worker so the inbox list doesn't aggregate per request), `email_inbound` (one row per received message, bodies stored inline, attachments metadata-only — file contents are NOT persisted in this slice; SendGrid Inbound Parse uploads are streamed to /dev/null), and extends `email_outbox` with `thread_id`, `provider_message_id` (Resend's internal id captured at send for visibility), `text_body` / `html_body` (lifted out of payload for the new admin-authored kinds), and `in_reply_to`. New outbox kinds `admin_outbound` (new conversation) and `admin_reply` (within existing thread) drain through the same LISTEN/NOTIFY worker; replies bump `last_outbound_at` + `outbound_count` on their thread. Threading resolves inbound messages via RFC 5322 In-Reply-To → outbox.provider_message_id / inbound.message_id → References chain (newest first) → normalised-subject fallback against active threads in the last 30 days. Webhook secret in path (`/webhooks/sendgrid-inbound/<secret>`, constant-time compared, 404 on miss); CSRF skipped for `/webhooks/*` since they're server-to-server with their own auth.), `0075_audit_subject_and_notes` (per-bettor audit visibility + operator notes — adds `admin_audit_log.subject_user_id UUID` (FK `users(id)` ON DELETE SET NULL) with a partial composite index `admin_audit_subject_idx (subject_user_id, created_at DESC) WHERE subject_user_id IS NOT NULL` so the new `GET /admin/users/:id/audit-log` filters in one indexed scan. Backfilled in-migration: every `target_type='user'` row gets `subject_user_id = target_id::uuid`; every `bettor_odds_adjustment_config` / `bettor_promo_visibility_config` row extracts the subject from the `user:<uuid>:…` prefix via `substring(target_id from 6 for 36)`. The audit-log hash chain (migration 0026) only hashes actor / action / target / before / after / ip / created_at — adding a column does NOT break the chain, the verifier is unaffected, and the new column is denormalised for query, not security. Also adds `users.notes TEXT` (CHECK ≤ 4000 chars) for free-text operator context surfaced on the RiskZilla bettor page; `PUT /admin/users/:id/notes` audit-logs as `user.notes_update`.), `0075_livesupport_chat` (live support chat between bettors and the backoffice — `support_threads` keyed by user with a partial unique index `support_threads_one_open_per_user` gating one open conversation per bettor at a time; denormalised `unread_user` / `unread_admin` counters on each row power the floating widget badge + the admin sidebar badge as O(1) reads (maintained inside the same tx as every message insert / mark-read so they can't drift away from the message log). `support_messages` is the append-only log with a `sender_kind` enum (`user` / `admin` / `system`) and a CHECK enforcing `sender_user_id IS NOT NULL` for any non-system row. Realtime fan-out rides the existing `user:{userId}` Redis pub/sub channel ws-gateway already forwards to authed sockets — no gateway changes; the front-end's shared WS dispatcher in `apps/web/src/lib/use-live-odds.ts` gains a `support_message` branch + `supportListeners` set, consumed by [`use-support-stream.ts`](./apps/web/src/lib/use-support-stream.ts). User-facing routes at [`services/api/src/modules/support/routes.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/support/routes.ts), admin routes at [`services/api/src/modules/admin/support.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/support.ts), floating widget at [`apps/web/src/components/support/support-widget.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/support/support-widget.tsx) mounted from (main) layout, admin pages at [`apps/web/src/app/admin/support/`](./apps/web/src/app/admin/support/) with a sidebar entry under Operations polling `/admin/support/unread-count` every 60 s.), `0082_zillabuild` (pre-built BetBuilder combos — `zillabuild_config` singleton (master on/off + eligible `provider_market_id` allowlist + cards-per-map / map-count / legs range + min combined-odds floor + response cache TTL) and `zillabuild_cards` per-(match, map, slot) card compositions (`legs` jsonb = `[{marketId, outcomeId}]`; odds re-quoted from OBB on read, never stored). Also extends the `bettor_promo_kind` enum from migration 0071 with a third value `zillabuild` so ZillaBuild joins the per-bettor promo-visibility cascade.), `0083_analytics` (first-party FE analytics — `analytics_sessions` (client-generated per-tab UUID; links to a user on the first authed flush; denormalised view/click/event counters), `analytics_events` (append-only journey log — page_view / click / heartbeat / session_end with a per-session monotonic `seq`, apply-once via `UNIQUE (session_id, seq)` so pagehide double-delivery never double-counts), `analytics_mouse_batches` (sampled ~8 Hz mouse trails as `[[dtMs,x,y]]` JSONB per flush with viewport dims; same seq counter space). `kind` / `section` are open TEXT on purpose (new event kinds without a migration — zillapass predicate_key rationale). Retention enforced by an hourly Redis-locked sweep in the api: 90 d sessions + events, 14 d mouse batches.)., `0084_fe_market_groups` (custom market groups + configurable tab order on the match-detail page — new `fe_market_groups` table keyed by `(sport_id, scope)`. Custom groups carry `scope='custom_<key>'` (random 12-hex key, stable across renames) + a NOT NULL `label` and behave exactly like the curated `top` scope: their market list lives in `fe_market_display_order` under the same scope string and renders as an extra tab with the operator-authored label. Built-in scopes (`match` / `top` / `map_<N>`) get label-NULL anchor rows that only carry `display_order` when the admin reorders tabs — tabs WITH a row sort first by `display_order`, the rest keep the default order (Top, Match, Map 1..N), so a sport with zero rows is byte-identical to pre-0083 behaviour. The `fe_market_display_order` scope CHECK is relaxed to accept `custom_<key>`. Admin API: `/admin/fe-settings/market-groups/:sportId` (list / create / rename / delete / PUT order — creating the first group or saving an order seeds anchor rows for every built-in tab so new groups append at the end); UI at `/admin/fe-settings/markets-order/[sportId]/groups`, linked from the scope chip strip + the landing table. Storefront: `/catalog/matches/:id` loads `fe_market_groups` in its parallel batch, synthesises custom groups through the same representative-pick helper as Top, and sorts the group array once — the web client needs no changes since `scopeLabel()` already falls through to `group.label` for unknown ids.), `0085_boosted_odds` (Custom Boosted Odds — `boosted_odds_config` rules pinned to one scope each: sport / tournament / match / competitor (team) / market, with `boost_pct NUMERIC(5,2)` (Netwinstable key-delta pp, same math as ZillaFlash), optional `ends_at`, optional `min_risk_score` (bettors with `users.risk_score` below the threshold see standard prices; anonymous viewers count as the 1.000 default). Partial unique per (scope, ref). Resolution per market: market > match > competitor > tournament > sport, competitor ties resolve to the higher pct. Boosted prices are recomputed from live `published_odds` on every read — never stored. Also adds `ticket_selections.boost_rule_id uuid` (FK ON DELETE SET NULL) so a boosted leg records the rule that priced it and the bet-delay worker skips per-leg drift for it — `odds_at_placement` is deliberately above the raw published price), `0086_zillaboost_banner` (adds `boosted_odds_config.banner BOOLEAN DEFAULT FALSE` — banner-enabled ZillaBoost rules surface on the storefront home page per scope: market → ZillaFlash-style offer card, match → scoreless match card with original + boosted match-winner prices, tournament → ZillaBoost banner linking to `/sport/:slug?tournament=N`, sport → **a home banner linking to `/sport/:slug` AND** the bolt icon next to the sport in the sidebar (banner added 2026-08-28 — before that, sport scope had the bolt only, so ticking "create promo banner" on a sport-wide boost appeared to do nothing; the lobby section also computed its "anything to show" total from tournament+match+market counts only, hiding the whole section for a sport-only boost). Sport + tournament banners deliberately carry NO odds — the scope spans every market of every match under it, so there is no single price to quote; the banner is a signpost and the list cards under it carry their own boosted prices. Neither is gated on `matchCount`; served by `GET /catalog/zillaboost-banners`, RS-gated per viewer), `0087_boosted_odds_outcome_scope` + `0088_boosted_odds_outcome_rules` (selection-level ZillaBoost — a rule may target ONE (market, outcome) cell instead of a whole market, so "Team Falcons to win map 2" can be boosted without moving the opposing price. 0087 does nothing but `ALTER TYPE boosted_odds_scope ADD VALUE 'outcome'` — deliberately its own file, because Postgres forbids REFERENCING a new enum value in the transaction that added it and the migration runner wraps each file in one tx, so 0088's `WHERE scope = 'outcome'` index predicate would fail with "unsafe use of new value of enum type". 0088 adds `outcome_id text` (reusing `market_id` for the other half of the `market_outcomes` PK), rebuilds `boosted_odds_scope_consistency` with the new tier (every pre-existing scope additionally pins `outcome_id IS NULL`), and adds the partial unique `boosted_odds_outcome_uniq (market_id, outcome_id) WHERE scope='outcome'` — which leaves `boosted_odds_market_uniq` untouched, so a market can carry a market rule AND per-outcome rules at once. **No FK on `(market_id, outcome_id)`** on purpose: validating one takes SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE on `market_outcomes`, which the feed writes to continuously, and the lock queue behind it is the failure mode 0023 was added to fix; the admin route checks the row exists on write and an outcome that later leaves the market just stops resolving. Math is `boostSelectionKeys` (netwinstable.ts): the delta comes out of the boosted outcome's own implied probability, clamped both by the fair-book floor and by `SELECTION_BOOST_MAX_PROB_SHARE` (half of that outcome's probability — the fair-book clamp alone can't bound one cell, since a market's headroom can exceed a longshot's entire probability and drive its price to infinity), with scarce headroom shared proportionally across co-boosted selections so the result is order-independent. A selection rule REPLACES any coarser rule on its market rather than stacking — composing them would push the book past fair). `0089_zillaboost_graphics_banner` (AI-generated ZillaBoost banner art — `boosted_odds_config.graphics_banner` flag + `zillaboost_banner_image_jobs` pull queue (rule_id PK, pending/done/failed, 15-min claim lease, hourly failure backoff, image BYTEA + mime on the job row so the hot rule-table selects never drag bytes). Drained by the operator-PC worker `services/zillaboost-banner-gen` via `/webhooks/banner-gen/:secret/*`; PC off = jobs wait), `0090_banner_gen_prompt` + `0091_banner_gen_render_meta` (`zillaboost_banner_image_jobs.last_prompt text` + `last_render_meta jsonb` — the diffusion prompt and the params behind it (checkpoint / latent class / cfg / steps / sampler / size / **seed** / negative). Image quality is iterated by changing prompts, and before these the prompt existed only in the operator PC's worker log, so "why does this Dota banner have soldiers in it" was unanswerable from the backoffice. The seed is the reproducibility handle. Both nullable + optional in the upload route so an older worker build still completes jobs; `/fail` records them too, COALESCEd so a failure report can't erase what a previous attempt stored), `0092_boosted_odds_starts_at` (**scheduled boosts** — `boosted_odds_config.starts_at timestamptz`; NULL = live immediately, which is what every pre-existing row means, so no backfill. A rule is deliverable only INSIDE its window, and the two halves are one shared predicate — [`boostWindowIsOpen()`](./services/api/src/lib/boosted-odds.ts) for SQL, `ruleWindowIsOpen()` in memory — because a reader that checked only `ends_at` would price, display, and PAY OUT a boost scheduled for next week. All four readers use it: both pricing loaders, `validateCustomBoostForBet` (rejects `boosted_odds_not_started`), and the banners endpoint. `boosted_odds_window_order` CHECK enforces `starts_at < ends_at` independently of the route. Deliberately NOT gated: the graphics job queue — generating artwork ahead of the start time is the point, so the image is ready when the boost goes live), `0093_boosted_odds_competitor_markets` (**team boost market span** — `boosted_odds_config.competitor_markets text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'all'` with CHECK `IN ('all','team_only')`. `all` = every market of the team's matches (the original behaviour, so the default leaves every existing row unchanged); `team_only` = only the team's own outcome in team-shaped markets (`provider_market_id IN (1,4)`), priced through the selection path so the opponent's price never moves. Meaningful only for `scope='competitor'` — left at the default and ignored elsewhere, the same convention `outcome_id` follows, rather than a CHECK that would need rebuilding every time a scope tier is added). `0094_tournament_risk_tier_lock` (**manual risk tier** — `tournaments.risk_tier_locked BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE`. TRUE when an operator assigned `risk_tier` on `/admin/tournaments`; feed-ingester's `UpdateTournamentRiskTier` skips locked rows so the REST refresh and the offline backfill never overwrite it. Exists because the Bifrost backup feed carries no risk tier: a tournament first seen while Oddin's meta API is down would otherwise sit at NULL and RiskZilla would price it off the tier-0 fallback. Default FALSE keeps every existing row on today's behaviour). `0095_feed_control` (**durable feed source switch** — singleton `feed_control` row: `source` auto/prod/backup, `switched_at`, `switched_by`, feed-ingester's `flushed_at` acknowledgement and `applied_source`/`applied_at`. Replaced the Redis `feed:source*` keys the same day they shipped, because `allkeys-lru` evicted them when the backup stream filled Redis and a forced Backup silently reverted to Auto). `0096_riskzilla_velocity_decision` + `0097_bet_intent_velocity` + `0098_behaviour_scores` (**bot controls**, 2026-09-03 — the answer to "can placement be restricted to a real click?" is no, so these make automation gain nothing and get noticed. 0096 adds the `rejected_velocity` value to `riskzilla_decision` in its own file (enum-in-same-tx rule, as 0087). 0097 adds the `riskzilla_bot_controls` singleton (intent token required / TTL, `min_human_ms`, velocity on/off + base caps for bets and distinct matches per minute, behaviour alert threshold + min sessions) and `tickets.quote_to_place_ms` — ms between the placement intent being issued and `POST /bets` landing. 0098 adds `analytics_sessions.behaviour_score / behaviour_features / behaviour_scored_at` plus the `bettor_behaviour_scores` per-bettor rollup with `alert` / `alert_since` / `acknowledged_at`.) `0099_feed_control_fonbet` (**Fonbet feed on/off switch** — adds `fonbet_enabled BOOLEAN NULL` (NULL = follow the `FONBET_ENABLED` env default; TRUE/FALSE = the operator's position, wins over env, survives restarts and deploys), `fonbet_switched_at` / `fonbet_switched_by`, and fonbet-ingester's acknowledgement `fonbet_applied_enabled` / `fonbet_applied_at` to the `feed_control` singleton. Written by `PUT /admin/feed/fonbet` (audit-logged `feed.fonbet_switch`), read every 2 s by fonbet-ingester's switch watcher: OFF suspends every Fonbet market (status -1, prices kept) and stops polling; ON boots the feed and re-activates whatever Fonbet still quotes. Card on `/admin/feed` via `GET /admin/feed/fonbet-status`, which also reads the service's Redis status hash `fonbet:feed:status`). `0100_sportradar_match_map` (**the third id space** — `match_sportradar_ids` (match_id PK → `matches`, `sr_match_id`, `sr_sport_id`, `status` candidate/confirmed/rejected, `source` admin/auto, `confidence`, `evidence` jsonb, reviewer + timestamps) plus the read-only view `match_external_ids` presenting every id we hold for a match in one shape. **The Oddin and Fonbet halves are DERIVED, not stored**: `matches.id` is already our own id for every fixture from every feed, `matches.provider_urn` already carries the provider's, and both prefixes are exactly 9 characters, so the view slices them with `substring(provider_urn FROM 10)` — copying them into a table could only ever drift. Sportradar is the only one that needs a row, because it is the only one no feed carries (Fonbet's events have no external-id field at all; Sportradar's gismo feed 403s off a licensed origin). `od:tournament:%` rows in `matches` (587 on 2026-09-04 — the auto-mapper's outright placeholders) are deliberately outside the view: they are not fixtures and have no Sportradar counterpart. The partial unique index on `sr_match_id` excludes `rejected`, so a tombstoned pair never blocks the correct match from claiming that id; a CHECK pins `confidence` to [0,1] and another pins reviewer-and-timestamp together. The file opens with `SET LOCAL lock_timeout = '5s'` because the FK takes a brief SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE on `matches`, which both ingesters write to continuously — failing the deploy beats queueing every feed write behind it.) `0101_sportradar_map_source_llm` (**adds `llm` to `sportradar_map_source`** — its own file, because Postgres forbids REFERENCING a new enum value in the transaction that added it, the same rule migration 0087 follows. The matcher queues what it cannot settle and nearly all of that turns out to be one provider abbreviating the other — Sportradar writes `Ipswich` where Fonbet writes `Ipswich Town`, two tokens against one, which scores 0.667 and drags an otherwise perfect pair (0-minute kickoff delta, identical away team) under the auto-confirm bar. No string metric fixes that: the missing ingredient is knowing that Ipswich Town is called Ipswich. A language model adjudicates the queue, and this value records that it was the deciding party — so the desk can show who decided, and so a bad batch is revertible with `WHERE source = 'llm'` without touching a matcher or human decision.) `0102_category_hidden_from_lists` (**a category can be carried in the tree but kept out of the match lists** — adds `categories.hidden_from_lists BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE`. Fonbet files EA FC simulations ("FC 26. ESportsBattle. La Liga. 2x4 min.") under the real Football sport, so 184 of ~1770 bookable Football matches — and 10 of 23 live ones — were computer-played 2x4-minute games sitting above the actual football offer; same shape as NBA 2K26 under Basketball and NHL 26 under Ice Hockey. A flagged category is dropped from the lobby, `/live`, `/upcoming`, the sport page's default view and the per-sport live badge, while STAYING in the sidebar tree — any explicit narrowing (`?category=`, `?tournament=`, `?team=`) drops the predicate, so the offer is one click away rather than gone. Seeds `TRUE` for `name ~ '^FC [0-9]{2}$'` (exactly `FC 24` + `FC 26` on the current line, verified against production before shipping); every other simulation category is an operator call on `/admin/categories`. Deliberately NOT `categories.active = false` — these matches are real, bettable and settle normally, so the merchandising decision and the kill switch stay independent). `0103_catalog_display_order` (**operator pin ordering for sports and categories** — adds a nullable `display_order INTEGER` to BOTH `sports` and `categories`. Until this, the sport rail was ordered by a hard-coded flagship list (cs2, dota2, lol, valorant) then alphabetically, and the sidebar's category buckets purely alphabetically, with no operator input at all — so Football's tree opened on Albania and the rail could not put Football above an esport the operator no longer leads with. NULL means unpinned, which is what every existing row gets, so ordering is byte-identical to before until an operator pins something; a pinned row sorts by this value ascending ahead of every unpinned one, and unpinned rows keep the old rule among themselves. **Scope differs by table and that is the point**: sports are one global sequence (a sport has no parent), categories one sequence PER SPORT (a category only ever renders inside one sport's tree, so “second from the top” is a statement about Football). The pinned set is stored dense 1..N and **renumbered on every action**, which is why the write goes through [`reorderPinned`](./services/api/src/lib/pin-order.ts) — a pure transform of the ordered id list, unit-tested — rather than arithmetic on one row: it makes the sequence self-healing against gaps and duplicates, and “up” / “down” are only definable relative to the other pinned rows anyway. **No index**: both tables are small enough (under a hundred sports, a few thousand categories) that an index would cost writes to serve a sort that is free. **No unique constraint** either — a deferred one would fight the renumber, and the single writer keeps the invariant. Endpoints `POST /admin/sports/:id/order` and `POST /admin/categories/:id/order` take `{action: top|up|down|clear}`, are audit-logged (`sport.order_update` / `category.order_update`, with the resulting `pinnedOrder` in `after`), and bust the caches their surface reads (`catalog:sports:v1` / `catalog:tournaments:v1:<sportId>`) so a reorder is not invisible for the cache TTL. Both take **every lock in one primary-key-ordered statement** — locking the clicked row and then the pinned set is two acquisitions in click-dependent order, which is exactly how two admins reordering the same scope deadlock each other). `0104_tournament_display_order` (**pin ordering for tournaments** — nullable `display_order` on `tournaments`, completing the three-level tree started by 0103. Scope is the CATEGORY, matching where the rows render: a tournament only appears inside one bucket, so “second from the top” is a statement about England. NULL = unpinned, so the tree is byte-identical until an operator pins something; same dense 1..N renumber, same single-statement primary-key-ordered lock, same absence of an index and a unique constraint as 0103). `0105_drop_russian_duplicate_categories` (**cleanup of the feed language switch** — the Fonbet line was read from fonbet.kz in Russian until 2026-09-04; moving to fon.bet in English created a fresh category row per country and orphaned the Russian one beside it (“Швеция” next to “Sweden”). Measured before writing: 211 such categories, 197 holding no tournament at all, the other 14 carrying 18 tournaments / 49 matches / 1054 markets — **none of it live offer** (every one of those matches has zero markets at status 1, so `hasActiveMarket` already hid them) with 0 settlements and 0 ticket_selections underneath. **Only the 197 EMPTY ones are deleted, and the FK is why**: the first cut deleted on the Cyrillic name alone and failed at deploy with `matches_tournament_id_fkey` — categories cascade to tournaments, but `matches.tournament_id` carries no on-delete action, so a tournament holding matches cannot be removed. That constraint is correct (a match is what tickets, settlements and odds history hang off, and it must not vanish because somebody tidied a category), so the migration was narrowed to fit it rather than the constraint being worked around; the transaction rolled back cleanly and nothing was half-applied. The remaining 14 stay in `/admin/categories` — they carry no bettable offer, so they cost a row in a list, and removing them means deleting real match rows with their own FK chain (`match_sportradar_ids`, `feed_messages`, `markets`, `odds_history` partitions), which deserves its own deliberate change. The `NOT EXISTS` ticket guard is kept even at a count of zero because the DELETE runs at deploy time, not authoring time). `0106_tournament_risk_tier_source` (**who decided a tournament's risk tier** — `tournaments.risk_tier_source` (`auto` / `manual` / `zagi`, CHECK-constrained TEXT rather than an enum so a fourth reviewer needs no two-file dance) plus `risk_tier_note`, `risk_tier_reviewed_at` and `risk_tier_attempts`. Until this there were two provenances and one bit to express them: `risk_tier_locked` (0094) meant an operator typed it, everything else was "auto" — which lumped *feed-assigned* together with *nobody has ever looked*, and 1 231 of 1 870 tournaments were the second kind. **The direction of the risk is what shapes the whole feature**: RiskZilla prices a NULL tier at `UNTIERED_RISK_TIER = 10`, the STRICTEST row in `riskzilla_settings` (50 USDC match liability against tier 1's 50 000), so an untiered competition is never over-exposed — it is invisible and under-traded, and every tier assigned to it LOOSENS the book. There is no cautious-by-omission assignment here, which is why the reviewer clamps in code rather than trusting the prompt and why an unparseable reply writes nothing. `risk_tier_attempts` is bounded retry (max 3) so a name the model will not judge stops being re-sent forever. No index — under 2 000 rows, same reasoning as 0103; `SET LOCAL lock_timeout = '5s'` because ADD COLUMN takes ACCESS EXCLUSIVE on a table both ingesters write to, and **a pre-deploy or 03:00-cron `pg_dump` holds AccessShareLock on every table for minutes**, so this migration aborts the deploy cleanly rather than queueing the catalog behind itself — retry once the dump finishes). `0106_fe_market_scope_sub_events` (**the match page's Fonbet sub-event tabs became configurable** — relaxes the `scope` CHECK on `fe_market_display_order` and `fe_market_groups` to accept `fb_<kinds>`: one scope per sub-event, id taken from the `variant` specifier (`fb:400100/10100201` → `fb_400100_10100201`), every per-player variant collapsing into `fb_players`. Those tabs — 1st half, Corners, Yellow cards, Players — have been on the storefront since the Fonbet line landed, but the backoffice enumerated a FIXED esports tab set instead, so it offered football Match plus Map 1..5, which no football fixture has ever had, while the tabs bettors were actually looking at could be neither ordered nor reordered. **The tab set is not stored anywhere** — the match page derives it per request from the markets it is about to render — so [`services/api/src/lib/fe-market-scopes.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/fe-market-scopes.ts) re-derives it over the sport's CURRENT offer (open matches, markets in a status the match page renders; the whole-table alternative reads ~17M rows in ~9 s and answers a different question — tabs from fixtures that finished months ago). Grammar and derivation live in ONE shared module, [`packages/types/src/market-scope.ts`](./packages/types/src/market-scope.ts), read by the api and the web admin and mirrored by these CHECKs, because a backoffice that disagrees with the storefront configures a tab nobody sees. Additive: no row changes, and a sport with no configuration keeps the order it has today). `0107_zagi_tier_safety_and_outright` (**the two operator tightenings, applied retroactively** — the first ZAGI sweep had already tiered 1 211 tournaments when the operator asked for a standing +1 safety margin on every machine verdict and a further +3 for outright markets. Both only ever raise the tier number, so this is a pure de-risking pass. The outright predicate mirrors `OUTRIGHT_PATTERNS` in the reviewer and was measured against the live catalogue first — deliberately NOT matching bare `head-to-head` (12 of 31 such rows are single-event: `Vuelta a Espana. Stage 13`, `Formula-1 … Race`) nor `champion` (`Scotland. Championship` is a league, not a market). Scope is `risk_tier_source = 'zagi'` only: an operator's tier is a human decision and a feed tier is Oddin's own, and neither is touched. **The `+1 ZAGI safety margin` marker in `risk_tier_note` is load-bearing, not decoration** — it is the statement's own idempotency key, so a row can never be stepped twice; verified against production in a rolled-back transaction where a second run touched 0 rows. Effect measured there: `England. Premier League. Season 26/27` T1 → T5, `Italy. Serie A. Season 26/27` T2 → T6, no ZAGI row left at T1). `0108_tournament_logo_provenance` (**where a tournament's logo came from** — `tournaments.logo_source` (`fonbet` / `wikidata` / `liquipedia` / `manual`, CHECK'd TEXT), `logo_source_url`, `logo_attempts` (bounded retry, max 3) and `logo_checked_at`. Backfills the 462 existing rows as `fonbet`, which is provably right because the feed is the only writer of tournament logos to date and every stored URL points at their CDN. **Provenance is load-bearing here rather than nice to have**: the remaining ~1 400 marks have to come from third parties, Liquipedia's are largely NON-FREE, and a single `WHERE logo_source = 'liquipedia'` has to be enough to undo the lot. Measured before writing: naive lookup is wrong often enough to matter — Wikidata's "EuroLeague" resolves to `EuroLeagueWomen.png` and Finland's "Liiga" to `Naisten Liiga logo.png` (the MEN'S entities carry no logo claim, so a sport-only filter falls through to the women's competition), and Liquipedia's search for "PGL Wallachia" returns the page **Team Falcons**, whose infobox image is a TEAM crest. A wrong badge is worse than a blank one). No migration for settlements retention (2026-07-03) — it's purely a cron: [`infra/hetzner/backup/settlements_retention.sh`](./infra/hetzner/backup/settlements_retention.sh) (`45 3 * * *`) deletes `settle`/`cancel` rows older than 45 days gated on the market having no open (`pending_delay`/`accepted`) ticket; `rollback_*` rows are kept forever. See invariant #3 and [`docs/OPERATIONS.md`](./docs/OPERATIONS.md) "settlements retention". |
| Drizzle schema | [`packages/db/src/schema/`](./packages/db/src/schema/) |
| Seed script | [`packages/db/src/seed.ts`](./packages/db/src/seed.ts) — seed sport URNs are `od:sport:{3,2,1,13}` matching real Oddin feed (do NOT revert to synthetic `od:sport:cs2` etc. — it breaks auto-mapping) |
| Money helpers | [`packages/types/src/money.ts`](./packages/types/src/money.ts) |
| Odds display formatting + bettability floor | [`packages/types/src/odds.ts`](./packages/types/src/odds.ts) — `formatOddsDisplay` (floor to 4dp, trim trailing zeros to a 2dp floor) + `ODDS_PLACEHOLDER`, plus `isBettableOdds` / `MIN_BETTABLE_ODDS` (`odds > 1`). The single formatter every bettor-facing price renders through: `OddButton` + the compact match-row button + the slip's per-leg odds. `isBettableOdds` is the shared "is this cell clickable" rule, mirroring the `authNum <= 1` reject in `POST /bets` so the UI never offers a price placement would refuse. Import via the `@oddzilla/types/odds` subpath, never the barrel. Do NOT use `formatBoostedOdds` (netwinstable.ts) for general display — that one floors to 2dp on purpose because a boosted quote is agreed to 2dp at placement. |
