| Recovery + alive-gap + alive-watchdog + handover sweeper | `services/feed-ingester/internal/handler/handler.go` (`TriggerRecovery`, `AliveState`, `mapFixtureStatus`, `handleMatchStatusChange`); `services/feed-ingester/cmd/feed-ingester/main.go` (`runHandoverSweeper`, `runRecoveryListener`, `runFixtureRefreshListener`, **`runAliveWatchdog`** + `feedSilence`). Two NOTIFY channels: `feed_recovery` (full AMQP replay) and `fixture_refresh` (single-URN REST refetch). No periodic phantom-drain — `<sport_event_status>` inside every `odds_change` is the lifecycle source of truth, and a stuck match is a real bug to surface, not noise to mop up. **`runAliveWatchdog` is the feed-down safety net**: when no AMQP message (alive heartbeat or odds_change) has arrived for `FEED_STALE_SUSPEND_SECONDS` (default 20s) it suspends the whole active catalog via `FlushAndSuspendActiveCatalog`, covering token-revocation / partition cases where `OnConnect`'s suspend-before-recover never runs because reconnects fail. `handleAlive`'s gap-detection only triggers recovery on alives that *arrive*; the watchdog is the absence-detector for alives that *stop*. |
| Live scoreboard (per-map score + current-map indicator) | `services/feed-ingester/internal/oddinxml/messages.go` (`SportEventStatus`, `PeriodScores`, `PeriodScore`, `Scoreboard` decode the `<sport_event_status>` block carried inside every `odds_change`); `services/feed-ingester/internal/handler/livescore.go` (`buildLiveScore`, `deriveCurrentMap` write the persisted JSON shape); `services/feed-ingester/internal/store/catalog.go` (`UpdateMatchLiveScore` writes `matches.live_score` jsonb); rendered by `apps/web/src/app/(main)/match/[id]/page.tsx` (`MapScoreboard` with per-map rounds/kills, current-map highlight + secondary scoreboard line for live map). Persisted JSON keeps top-level `home`/`away` for back-compat with the list-card `match-row.tsx`. See [`docs/ODDIN.md`](./docs/ODDIN.md#live-scoreboard-sport_event_status). |
| Match lifecycle apply (forward-only) | `services/feed-ingester/internal/store/catalog.go` (`UpdateMatchStatus` — SQL guard rejects regressions from `closed`/`cancelled` and to `not_started`; returns `(changed bool, err)` so the caller can broadcast only on genuine transitions, not idempotent re-applies; `UpdateAllMarketsStatusForMatch` exists for the rarely-emitted `bet_stop groups="all"`); `services/feed-ingester/internal/handler/handler.go` (`handleOddsChange` forwards every `<sport_event_status status="…"/>` code through `UpdateMatchStatus` so `not_started → live → closed/cancelled` transitions land in real time, and publishes a `matchStatus` WS frame on the `odds:match:{id}` channel whenever the row actually moves — same channel as odds + market-status ticks, ws-gateway forwards the JSON verbatim; `handleBetStop` and `handleMatchStatusChange` are no-ops for bookmaker 142 since the broker doesn't emit those types — kept as graceful handlers for bookmakers that do); `services/settlement/internal/store/store.go` (`MarkMatchClosedIfAllMarketsTerminal` — flips match to `closed` when no market remains at `1/0/-1/-2`, returns `(matchID, closed, err)`, called from both `handleBetSettlement` and `handleBetCancel` which publish the same `matchStatus` frame on close so the storefront drops the LIVE pill the moment Oddin stops re-asserting an active state — Oddin sometimes drops the final `<sport_event_status status="4">` once the last market settles, so this path is the safety net the WS pipeline rides). Storefront fan-out: [`apps/web/src/lib/use-live-odds.ts`](./apps/web/src/lib/use-live-odds.ts) (`useLiveMatchStatus` / `useLiveMatchStatusForMatches`) merged into the rendered `match.status` by `match-list-tabs.tsx`'s `mergeMatchWithLive` (sport / lobby / live / upcoming lists), the new [`MatchHeaderStatusPill`](./apps/web/src/components/match/header-status-pill.tsx) on both match-detail pages, `LiveScoreboard` (drops current-map highlight on close), `MatchLiveMedia` (hides the live-stats widget on close), and `MatchPageRegistrar` (republishes the live status into `MatchPageContext` so the rail's `RailMatchPanel` follows the transition without a hard reload). No background reconciliation: a match that drifts out of sync with the AMQP feed is a real bug to debug, not noise to paper over. |
| Bifrost backup feed (odds + settlement standby) | [`services/bifrost-feed/`](./services/bifrost-feed/) — consumes Oddin's Bifrost GraphQL API (the white-label esports front end behind `maxbet.rs/en/esport`; Oddin authorised MaxBet's brand key, client 101, on 2026-09-03; key in `BIFROST_API_KEY`, never in the repo). One `graphql-transport-ws` socket, one `onUpdateMatchLive` subscription per match on the active offer plus the global `onMatchStateChanged` stream; [`internal/translate`](./services/bifrost-feed/internal/translate/) renders every snapshot as byte-compatible Oddin `odds_change` (market OPEN/SUSPENDED → 1/-1; outcome OPEN → active; `probabilities` = margin-normalised 1/odds; `simpleScore` → `period_scores` with rounds / kills / goals by sport family + `scoreboard` for the live period) and `bet_settlement` (WON/LOST → result 1/0, HALF_* → void_factor 0.5, VOIDED → void_factor 1) and publishes onto the `oddin.backup` Redis stream. **Settlement is a comparison of two current states, not a log**: a market is voiced only when Bifrost shows it CLOSED with terminal outcomes AND our `markets.status` is still non-terminal ([`internal/dbstate`](./services/bifrost-feed/internal/dbstate/)), so restarts, reconnects and the primary's 24 h replay converge without coordination. [`internal/gate`](./services/bifrost-feed/internal/gate/) reads two feed-ingester stamps — `feed:primary:last_msg_unix` (every AMQP delivery, at most once per second) and `feed:primary:connected_unix` (every 2 s with a 15 s TTL while the AMQP connection is open, deleted on disconnect via the consumer's `OnDisconnect` hook) — and counts the primary alive while EITHER is fresh: `BIFROST_MODE=auto` publishes after `BIFROST_TAKEOVER_AFTER_SECONDS` (45 s, past the 20 s watchdog suspend) with both stale and stands down the instant one is fresh. The connection stamp exists because the first production restart (2026-09-03) left the connection open but delivery-free for over two minutes (76 s OnConnect flush + 80 s Oddin replay ramp) and the backup took over for 33 s on a healthy feed; on activation every cached snapshot is re-emitted so the suspended catalogue comes back in one pass; `active` forces, `off` idles, empty key idles. **Operator switch**: the **Feed source** control on `/admin/feed` (Backup is branded **CommZilla**, "Communism Mode", in the backoffice; the stored value, the env vars and the service name are all still `backup` / `bifrost-feed`, so the joke lives only in operator-facing copy) (`PUT /admin/feed/source`, Postgres singleton `feed_control` row (migration 0095) = `auto` / `prod` / `backup`, audit-logged, `backup` needs a confirm string) overrides the env default. **Never move this state to Redis**: production Redis is `maxmemory 256mb` + `allkeys-lru`; the first cut's Redis keys were evicted on day one when the backup stream (then MAXLEN 50k × ~35–150 KB snapshots) filled it, a forced Backup silently reverted to Auto, and the stream is now trimmed by time (`XADD … MINID` 60 s). The two liveness stamps stay in Redis because they are refreshed every 1–2 s. `backup` is a real source switch: feed-ingester's `runSourceSwitch` **stops dialling Oddin's broker entirely** (`amqp.Consumer.Paused`, gated on `feedSourceIsBackup`, polled every 2 s so switching back reconnects promptly) and acks any in-flight deliveries without applying them. It used to hold the connection open and merely drop deliveries, on the reasoning that transport liveness stamps kept the alive watchdog quiet — but the watchdog's forced-Backup branch guards bifrost-feed's health and never reads AMQP anyway, so the connection bought nothing. What it cost was visible on 2026-09-04: with the Oddin credentials returning `403 username or password not allowed`, a source the operator had switched off a day earlier was still dialled every 30 s forever. A paused consumer logs the transition, not the poll, suspends the catalogue once and acknowledges with `feed:source:flushed_unix`; bifrost-feed activates only after that acknowledgement (15 s ceiling) so its full re-emit lands on the clean slate; switching back runs the reconnect-style flush + 24 h Oddin replay. Settlement is deliberately outside the switch (dual-source always; apply-once makes it safe and cancel / rollback exist only on AMQP). **Backup means Bifrost only, for the feed** (operator decision 2026-09-03): while forced, feed-ingester makes NO Oddin feed REST call — `automap.Resolver.WithRESTGate` sends fixtures straight to Bifrost and skips tournament-info / competitor-profile fetches, `handler.Deps.RestAllowed` skips recovery requests, the descriptions refresh and competitor backfill are skipped, an AMQP reconnect neither flushes nor replays, and settlement skips its OnConnect recovery request; the alive watchdog guards bifrost-feed's heartbeat + socket (`backupHealthy`) instead of AMQP. Labels then come from Bifrost: the translator sets `<market name=…>` (a non-Oddin attribute; `store.SeedMarketDescriptionIfMissing`, DO NOTHING so REST overwrites) and `<outcome name=…>` (real Oddin attribute → `market_outcomes.name`; player URNs also seed `player_profiles`), and the catalog's competitor / player label chain falls back to that per-instance name before the raw URN. Switching back re-enables REST, replays, and refreshes descriptions + competitor profiles immediately. While active, every resync re-emits all snapshots and sweeps 3 h of results (24 h on activation and every sixth resync) so a long outage of the whole stack heals without manual steps. **Bifrost states a `handicap` specifier from the OPPOSITE side to the AMQP feed, so `bifrost.NormalizeSpecifiers` flips its sign in `parseMarketSegment`** — the one place a market key is built, which is why the correction covers odds and settlement together and the storefront needs no change (it renders the line from the specifier: home as-is, away negated). Measured 2026-09-04: every backup-fed market keyed `handicap=-1.5` priced the HOME outcome SHORTER than that team's own moneyline (CS2 1163395 — moneyline 1.55, "-1.5" 1.15, "+1.5" 2.70), which a real -1.5 line can never be; correct score agreed to two decimals. Systematic across all nine handicap-bearing market ids (100% inverted on backup vs 100% standard on AMQP, flipping the day the source switched). **This had to be fixed at key level, not in the label**: identity is `(match, provider_market_id, specifiers_hash)` and settlement is dual-source, so an inverted key means Oddin's `bet_settlement` grades the OPPOSITE line of the one the backup priced — 19 of the 26 backup-era margin-1 ±1.5 pairs were graded on the AMQP convention, 4 on Bifrost's, 3 contradictory (no money moved: one OZ demo ticket, no handicap leg). Oddin's own AMQP feed carried the same inversion until 2026-06-02, so Bifrost is serving the pre-fix convention — if they ever align, the flip has to come back out, and the moneyline test above is what pins the direction. `handicap` is the only specifier key that ever holds a negative value. Consumers: [`services/feed-ingester/internal/backupstream`](./services/feed-ingester/internal/backupstream/consumer.go) and [`services/settlement/internal/backupstream`](./services/settlement/internal/backupstream/consumer.go) (XREADGROUP, per-service group, routing key `bifrost.backup`; they call the same `Handle` the AMQP path uses and must never bump `lastAmqpMessageUnix`). **Two things about finishing matches, both fixed 2026-09-03 after production stranded them.** (1) Once a match ends, Bifrost answers `match(historic: false)` with a real object (`state: CLOSED`) and an EMPTY `marketGroups`; every settled market is only in the `historic: true` view (measured: 0 vs 532-607 CLOSED markets on three CS2 matches). `Client.FetchMatch` asks both and returns whichever carries markets — returning the first non-nil result fed the results sweep a market-less snapshot forever, so it logged `matches_settled: 0` on every pass while its fetch list grew unbounded. (2) Nothing else closes the match: it leaves the live offer so its subscription goes quiet, and settlement's `MarkMatchClosedIfAllMarketsTerminal` needs EVERY market row we hold to go terminal, so one market Bifrost no longer lists strands it at `live` forever. `translate.OddsChange` therefore emits a lifecycle-only `odds_change` (`sport_event_status status=4`, no `<odds>` block) for a CLOSED match, the results sweep publishes it too, and feed-ingester's `applyLifecycleOnly` applies it — terminal codes only, existing matches only, so a market-less message can never auto-create a fixture. A LIVE match with an all-closed book still says nothing: that is an ordinary between-rounds suspension. Fixture metadata without the REST meta API: [`services/feed-ingester/internal/bifrost`](./services/feed-ingester/internal/bifrost/client.go) reshapes Bifrost's `match` query into `oddinxml.FixtureResponse`; `automap.Resolver.fetchFixtureAny` tries REST then Bifrost and seeds `competitor_profiles` icons. Admin card on `/admin/feed` via `GET /admin/feed/backup-status` (Redis hash `bifrost:feed:status`). `-dry-run` exercises the whole path with no Postgres / Redis. **Deliberate gaps (2026-09-03):** no `bet_cancel` / `rollback_*` synthesis (Bifrost has no cancel state; a cancelled event shows as CLOSED with markets removed — deferred until Oddin confirms the representation), probabilities derived from odds, risk tier assigned manually (migration 0094). Full protocol notes, enum evidence, recovery interplay and runbook: [`docs/BIFROST_BACKUP_FEED.md`](./docs/BIFROST_BACKUP_FEED.md); operation catalogue: [`docs/fixtures/bifrost-operations.graphql`](./docs/fixtures/bifrost-operations.graphql). |
| Tournament risk tier (manual assignment + lock) | [`services/api/src/modules/admin/tournaments.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/tournaments.ts) — `PATCH /admin/tournaments/:id` accepts `riskTier: 1..10` (sets `risk_tier` AND `risk_tier_locked=true`) or `riskTier: null` (unlocks; value stays until the next REST refresh replaces it), audit-logged; list returns `riskTierLocked`. **The list is filterable and sortable** (2026-09-05): `?sportId`, `?categoryId` (scoped to the sport — football alone carries ~200 country buckets, so an unscoped dropdown would be unusable), `?tier` (1..10 or `unset`, which is a first-class choice since an untiered row is the one an operator most wants to find), `?source` (`auto` / `zagi` / `manual`), plus `?sort=` name / sport / category / tier / source with `?dir=`. Sort keys are an allowlist mapped to columns, never a column name off the query string; every sorted query carries a `name, id` tiebreak so paging cannot repeat or skip a row, and an untiered row sorts to the END whichever way the tier column points, because "no tier" is neither a low tier nor a high one. Clicking an already-descending column returns to the DEFAULT pin order — otherwise that arrangement, the only one in which the reorder arrows mean anything, would be unreachable without clearing every filter. One shared `filtersToQuery` builds every link, because the pager previously assembled its own params and would have silently dropped the new filters on page 2. Since 0106 a manual assignment also stamps `risk_tier_source='manual'` and clears any ZillaAGI note (it explained a number the operator just replaced), and reverting to Auto restores the label the VALUE deserves — `zagi` when a review timestamp exists, `auto` otherwise, because crediting the feed with a decision it never made is how the "auto" bucket became unreadable in the first place. UI: Risk tier column on [`/admin/tournaments`](./apps/web/src/app/admin/tournaments/tournaments-editor.tsx) (`T<n> manual` / `ZAGI` / `Auto`); a tournament with NO tier renders a highlighted `unset` chip there and a highlighted `T?` via `TierBadge` on the ZillaBoost board, because that row is actionable rather than merely unknown. feed-ingester's `store.UpdateTournamentRiskTier` adds `AND NOT risk_tier_locked`, so neither the per-fixture refresh nor `-backfill-tournament-metadata` overwrites an operator's choice. **An untiered tournament is underwritten at the STRICTEST tier, not the loosest.** `UNTIERED_RISK_TIER = 10` in [`services/api/src/lib/riskzilla/engine.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/riskzilla/engine.ts) is what `tierFor` resolves a NULL `risk_tier` to (tier 10 = 50 USDC match liability / 25 USDC max payout, versus tier 0's 5000 / 1000). Before 2026-09-03 a NULL fell through to the tier-0 global fallback, which is one of the loosest rows in the table — so a tournament first seen while Oddin's meta API was down was underwritten as if it were a mid-tier event. Bifrost carries no tier at all, which makes that reachable in normal operation, not just in an outage. Fail-safe until an operator assigns a real tier. |
| ZillaAGI (ZAGI) — the in-house LLM | Client [`services/api/src/lib/zagi/client.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/zagi/client.ts). One OpenAI-compatible chat endpoint (`https://llm.oddin.gg/v1`, model `glm-5.3-flash`) behind `ZAGI_BASE_URL` / `ZAGI_API_KEY` / `ZAGI_MODEL`, falling back to the `SPORTRADAR_LLM_*` trio that named the same gateway first, so an existing deployment gains ZAGI without an `.env` edit. Three things the client exists to stop each caller rediscovering: the base URL **includes** the `/v1` (it posts to `<base>/chat/completions` and appends nothing); the model must be **pinned**, because `/v1/models` lists more than one and auto-discovery takes an arbitrary first entry; and a reasoning model spends most of `max_tokens` on an internal pass before emitting a character, so `complete()` surfaces `finishReason` + token counts and throws a typed `ZagiEmptyReplyError` rather than returning "" — an exhausted budget and a model with nothing to say need different operator responses. Graceful-idle: no key, no client, and every feature built on it no-ops. |
| ZAGI tournament risk-tier review | Engine [`services/api/src/lib/zagi/risk-tier.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/zagi/risk-tier.ts) (prompt, `renderBatch`, `parseTierVerdicts`, `clampTier`, `assignRiskTiers`, `riskTierBacklog`), sweeper [`sweeper.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/zagi/sweeper.ts) (30 min, Redis NX lock, boot-delayed, `ZAGI_RISK_TIER_DISABLED=1` skips; a sweep takes 200-row chunks until the queue is empty or a **10-minute budget** is spent, because a fixed rows-per-sweep cap is the wrong shape for a backlog — 200 every 30 min would take three and a half hours to clear a queue the model works through in ten minutes, while the steady state of a few new tournaments finishes on the first chunk. It stops early on no progress, since a transport failure burns no attempt and looping would just repeat it. The Redis lock's TTL floors at the budget plus slack rather than a fraction of the interval, or a second replica would take over mid-sweep at the 5-minute minimum interval), unit tests [`risk-tier.test.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/zagi/risk-tier.test.ts). The prompt tells the model the ceilings AND explicitly NOT to make the safety or outright adjustments itself, so the two halves compose rather than doubling. Admin `GET /admin/tournaments/zagi-status` + `POST /admin/tournaments/zagi-review` (`dryRun` previews, `sportId` scopes, 20/hour, audit-logged as `tournament.zagi_review`, busts `catalog:tournaments:v1:<sportId>`), UI panel at the top of [`/admin/tournaments`](./apps/web/src/app/admin/tournaments/tournaments-editor.tsx). **The model proposes and code disposes** — `resolveTier` applies three tightenings, and every one of them can only RAISE the tier number (lower the liability), never lower it. (1) **+1 always** (`SAFETY_MARGIN_STEPS`, operator decision 2026-09-05): a ZAGI verdict is not reviewed by a person before it takes effect, so it is not taken at face value — if the model says tier 2, the book underwrites tier 3. The consequence worth stating out loud is that **ZAGI can never assign T1**; the loosest tier in the book, 50 000 USDC on one match, is reachable only by an operator typing it. (2) **+3 for OUTRIGHT markets** (`OUTRIGHT_TIER_STEPS`): an outright resolves over a whole season or phase rather than one fixture, so the book holds the position for months, cannot trade out of it match by match, and prices it off standings that move underneath it. Before this, `England. Premier League. Season 26/27` sat at the SAME tier as the Premier League's match markets. `OUTRIGHT_PATTERNS` was measured against the live catalogue, and the two near-misses are the interesting part: bare `head-to-head` is EXCLUDED because in this feed it is dominated by single-event markets (`Vuelta a Espana. Stage 13. Head-to-head`, `Formula-1. Grand Prix. Italy. Race. Head-to-head` — 12 of 31 rows), and `champion` is excluded because it matches `Scotland. Championship` and `Gaelic football. Galway Championship`, which are ordinary leagues. (3) The per-sport ceiling: `SPORT_TIER_CEILING` is the operator's own rule made mechanical — the tier measures a competition's standing in WORLD sport, not inside its own sport, so only football / basketball / tennis / american-football may reach T1, handball and the other mid-size sports stop at T2, real-but-narrow circuits (cycling, darts, snooker, table tennis, badminton, futsal, chess) at T3, and anything unlisted at a cautious T4. `clampTier` can only ever RAISE a number, never lower one, so a model that proposes something stricter than the ceiling is taken at its word. A second floor at T9 catches simulations: Fonbet files FC 26 under Football and NBA 2K26 under Basketball, so the sport ceiling alone would let a machine playing itself on a 2x4-minute clock through at T1 — `SIMULATED_PATTERNS` anchors on game titles, known simulated-league brands and the short clock (accepting Cyrillic `х`, which the English catalogue mixes in), and deliberately omits bare "Liga Pro", a real table-tennis circuit as well as a Fonbet FC bracket. Eligibility is narrow — no tier, not locked, active, under `MAX_REVIEW_ATTEMPTS` — and every UPDATE re-asserts it, so an operator who assigns a tier while the model is thinking wins. A transport failure burns no attempt (it is our problem, not the row's); only a reply that ignored the row does. Measured on the real backlog before shipping: 45/45 decided in 14 s, ceilings respected, simulated caught, and a prompt-injection probe planted in a tournament name was ignored and rated T9. |
| Settlement payout math + tests | `services/settlement/internal/settler/payout.go` |
| Settlement workflows (settle + cancel + rollbacks + cancel-after-settle + per-generation ledger refs) | `services/settlement/internal/settler/settler.go`; `services/settlement/internal/store/store.go` (`nextPayoutRefID`, `LatestUnreversedPayoutRefID`) |
| Stranded-ticket reconciler (settle path missed a leg-result write) | `settler.ReconcileStranded` + `store.HealStrandedSelections` / `store.SettleReadyAcceptedTickets`; periodic sweeper `runReconcileSweeper` in `services/settlement/cmd/settlement/main.go` (`SETTLEMENT_RECONCILE_INTERVAL_SECONDS`, default 300, <=0 disables). The per-message cascade (`ApplyOutcomeToSelections`) only writes a leg's `result` on the FRESH settle (`InsertIfNew inserted=true`); a replay skips it, so a selection that became settle-eligible after that pass (ticket still in bet-delay, recovery message reorder) stayed `result IS NULL` on an already-terminal market → ticket stuck `accepted` forever (diagnosed 2026-06-01). The reconciler heals NULL legs from the authoritative current `market_outcomes.result`/`void_factor` on a terminal (`status ∈ {-3,-4}`) market for still-`accepted` tickets, then settles any now-fully-resolved ticket via the normal phase-2 path. Reads current DB truth, never message replays, so a rolled-back market (`status=1`, NULL result) is never touched and the replay-gated rollback path can't be defeated. The `applyMarketSettle` `inserted` gate is intentionally left unchanged for exactly this reason. **The heal UPDATE had never actually run until 2026-09-04**: it joined `tickets` in the FROM clause on `t.id = ts.ticket_id`, and Postgres forbids referencing the UPDATE target from a FROM-clause join condition, so every sweep failed with `42P01 invalid reference to FROM-clause entry for table "ts"` and logged `reconcile sweep failed`. The ticket-status check is an EXISTS subquery now. Anything that writes to a table it is also filtering through a join needs the same care. |
| Bet-delay evaluator + tests | `services/bet-delay/internal/worker/worker.go` |
| Chain scanners (Go) | `services/wallet-watcher/internal/{ethereum,tron}/`; shared confirmation tick in `internal/deposits/` |
| Frontend live-odds + ticket WS | [`apps/web/src/lib/use-live-odds.ts`](./apps/web/src/lib/use-live-odds.ts), [`use-ticket-stream.ts`](./apps/web/src/lib/use-ticket-stream.ts) |
| WS socket identity vs session | [`apps/web/src/lib/ws-session-sync.tsx`](./apps/web/src/lib/ws-session-sync.tsx) — mounted in the `(main)` layout, publishes the SSR-resolved user id into the shared socket via `setExpectedSessionUser()`. The gateway authenticates ONCE at upgrade (invariant 7), so a socket opened logged-out stays anonymous through a login and never receives `user:{id}` ticket frames; the same happens when a tab reconnects after a gateway restart with an already-expired 15-minute access cookie. On `hello.userId !== expectedUserId` the connection closes itself and the reconnect re-reads the cookie, capped at `MAX_AUTH_RECONNECTS` so an expired cookie (only a navigation through the Next.js middleware refreshes it) can't spin. |
| Ticket resolution during the bet-delay window | [`bet-slip-rail.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/shell/bet-slip-rail.tsx) — `applyTicketResolution()` is fed by TWO transports: the `user:{id}` WS frame (fast path) and an HTTP poll of `GET /bets/:id` starting just after `notBeforeTs`. The poll is not redundancy for its own sake: when the frame never arrived the button sat on "Placing…", timed out, and reverted to "Place bet" **with the slip still populated** while the bet was already accepted and the stake debited. Nothing upstream catches the resulting second click — every submit mints a fresh `idempotencyKey`, so the retry is a genuinely new ticket. Observed in production 2026-09-03. |
| Frontend bet slip store | [`apps/web/src/lib/bet-slip.tsx`](./apps/web/src/lib/bet-slip.tsx) |
| Bet slip right-rail UI | [`apps/web/src/components/shell/bet-slip-rail.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/shell/bet-slip-rail.tsx) |
| Shell components | [`apps/web/src/components/shell/`](./apps/web/src/components/shell/) — `top-bar.tsx`, `top-bar-search.tsx` (debounced global search popover), `sidebar.tsx` (auto-expands tournament sub-tree under the active sport), `bet-slip-rail.tsx`, `theme-toggle.tsx`, `zillapass-indicator.tsx` (signed-in-only progress chip beside the search bar — polls `GET /zillapass/me` every 30 s, renders `completed/total` + a mini progress bar; click opens a popover with per-task progress and an "Open ZillaPass" link to the full page) |
| ZillaPass (TS, scaffold) | API: [`services/api/src/modules/zillapass/routes.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/zillapass/routes.ts) (`GET /zillapass/me` — bundles active tasks + per-period progress + level / xp / streak) + [`services/api/src/modules/admin/zillapass.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/zillapass.ts) (admin task CRUD; audit-logged). Schema: [`packages/db/src/schema/zillapass.ts`](./packages/db/src/schema/zillapass.ts), migration `0060_zillapass`. Storefront: [`apps/web/src/components/shell/zillapass-indicator.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/shell/zillapass-indicator.tsx) + [`apps/web/src/app/(main)/zillapass/`](./apps/web/src/app/(main)/zillapass/). Admin: [`apps/web/src/app/admin/zillapass/`](./apps/web/src/app/admin/zillapass/) (linked under **Storefront → ZillaPass**). Shared types: [`packages/types/src/zillapass.ts`](./packages/types/src/zillapass.ts). **Storefront i18n**: task titles / descriptions / CTA labels are DB rows (seeded in English), so the storefront translates them per-slug via `zillapass.taskCatalog` in [`apps/web/messages/*.json`](./apps/web/messages/) through the [`useZillapassTaskText`](./apps/web/src/lib/zillapass-i18n.ts) helper — slugs without a catalog entry (admin-created tasks) fall back to the DB English text, and operator edits to seeded wording must be mirrored into the message files or every locale keeps showing the catalog copy. No service writes progress today — predicate hooks land in a follow-up once the product team finalises task kinds. |
| Tournament logo sourcing (Wikidata + Liquipedia) | [`services/api/src/lib/tournament-logos/`](./services/api/src/lib/tournament-logos/) — `resolver.ts` (ZAGI canonical-name prompt + strict parser + orchestration + byte download), `wikidata.ts`, `liquipedia.ts`, `sweeper.ts` (hourly, Redis-locked, **time**-budgeted at 10 min because Liquipedia asks 2 s between calls), guards unit-tested in `wikidata.test.ts`. Admin `GET /admin/tournaments/logo-status` + `POST /admin/tournaments/logo-fetch` (`dryRun` previews, 10/hour, audit-logged). Migration 0108. **Why third parties at all:** Fonbet's `line/logos` catalogue is fully consumed at 462 rows — that is everything they have — and the Oddin esports half has no first-party source, because the Oddin REST token 403s and the stack has run on the Bifrost backup feed since 2026-09-03, whose `Tournament` type exposes `sport { icon }` and no tournament icon. **The split of labour is the design:** ZAGI supplies the canonical competition name plus up to two aliases (nothing local knows `Spain. Primera Division` is La Liga, that `ESL Challenger League Season 52: Europe - Cup #6` is an instance whose logo belongs to the series, or that LCK is also `League of Legends Champions Korea`), and code then does an EXACT lookup and refuses anything whose page title or entity label does not match the name asked for. That guard is the whole safety story, and every case in it is a real observed failure: `EuroLeague` → `EuroLeagueWomen.png`, Finland's `Liiga` → `Naisten Liiga logo.png` (the men's entities carry no logo claim, so a sport-only filter falls through to the women's competition), `Eredivisie` → the Dutch ICE HOCKEY league, and Liquipedia's *search* for `PGL Wallachia` → the page **Team Falcons**, whose infobox image is a team crest — which is why pages are looked up by title and never searched. Bytes are downloaded and stored in `logo_data` rather than hot-linked (Commons and Liquipedia are not our CDN; the storefront already serves `/api/tournaments/:id/logo`, and the api's `default-src 'none'` CSP is what makes serving third-party SVG safe). Liquipedia leads for the esports slugs that have a wiki and Wikidata for everything else. **Measured, not estimated:** 60% of the tournaments that could plausibly have a logo, landing the marquee events (The International, IEM, DreamLeague, LEC, Esports World Cup, MLB, Premier League, La Liga); the misses are county championships, third divisions and simulated fixtures that have a logo nowhere, which ZAGI correctly declines rather than guessing at. |
| Match list ordering (prominence, then time) | `matchListOrder()` in [`services/api/src/modules/catalog/routes.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/catalog/routes.ts), applied by `/catalog/sports/:slug` and `/catalog/matches` (lobby, `/live`, `/upcoming`). Until 2026-09-05 the sport page sorted live-before-upcoming then purely by kickoff, and `/catalog/matches` sorted the live tab by `matches.id DESC` — which is insertion order. On a line as broad as Fonbet's that means the top of Football's live list was `Venezuela. Division 2` and `Brazil. Women. Series A1` while Brazil's Serie A sat below the fold: the ordering carried no notion of which match anyone wants. Two operator rules now: **(1)** among matches competing for the top, LOWER `risk_tier` ranks higher — the tier is already our best statement of how big a competition is, and it finally exists for the traditional line too (ZillaAGI, migration 0106); **(2)** a **tier 1 or 2** match joins that competition **12 hours before kickoff**, so an upcoming US Open singles outranks a live MLB game. Everything else stays chronological — a "what's on soon" list sorted by prestige would be actively worse — with the tier only breaking ties. Untiered rows sort as 99: last within their group, never promoted by missing information. `HOISTABLE_TIERS` is deliberately narrower than `isFeaturedTier` (1..3): a gold star is decoration, displacing live football is a merchandising claim. Verified against production before shipping — Football's live list led with Serie A (T4) instead of Venezuela Division 2, and five upcoming US Open (T2) fixtures hoisted above live T3 MLB. |
| UI primitives | [`apps/web/src/components/ui/`](./apps/web/src/components/ui/) — `primitives.tsx` (Button, Pill, LiveDot, Tabs, OddButton, TeamMark, which renders the crest when `logoUrl` is set and NOTHING otherwise or on `<img onError>` — the monogram-of-initials fallback was removed 2026-09-05 because "KY/" beside a tennis doubles pair read as a fake badge; two-row surfaces (match list, scoreboard) hold an empty same-sized slot when only one side has a picture so the names stay flush, Divider), `icons.tsx`, `monogram.tsx`, `sport-glyph.tsx`, `tier-mark.tsx` (gold "Top" star + `isFeaturedTier()` predicate, **`risk_tier` 1..3 since 2026-09-05** — widened from `{1,2}` because ZillaAGI now tiers the traditional line too and its standing +1 safety margin means a machine verdict can never produce a T1, which left the whole Fonbet offer with four featured tournaments between them; three tiers restores a meaningful Top set on both halves of the catalogue without an operator having to raise a competition's liability budget just to put a star on it; rendered next to the tournament name on match cards, the sidebar tournament tree, top-bar search hits, and the match-detail header. Featured cards also pick up a 2px gold left-edge accent and a "TOP" pill on the detail page; both tiers share one visual treatment per the user's "просто Top для обоих этих тиров" call) |
| Market names carry their sub-event (all sports) | [`services/api/src/modules/catalog/routes.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/catalog/routes.ts) — Fonbet ships a sub-event market as `"<sub-event>: <market>"` (`"3rd set aces: Match result"`). The route splits that prefix into the match page's scope TAB, and until 2026-09-05 it also stripped it from `market.name`, on the reasoning that the tab already says it. **That reasoning only holds where the tab is on screen.** `market.name` is what the bet slip stores as `marketLabel` (see the `add()` call in [`live-markets.tsx`](./apps/web/src/app/(main)/match/[id]/live-markets.tsx)), what bet history renders, and what a copied community ticket shows — none of which carry a tab. Observed on production: a leg on the 3rd-set-ACES three-way rendered in the slip as `MATCH RESULT / 1 / Michelsen A vs Merida Aguilar D` at 2.45, indistinguishable from a match-winner bet and priced nothing like one. The prefix now stays on the name; only `baseName` (the small market-kind tag, and the line-grouping key) is still stripped. Repetition inside one tab is a cosmetic cost, an unidentifiable leg on a money surface is not. Oddin markets were never affected — their templates already carry the map (`"Total kills {threshold} - map {map}"`), and this was the only stripping path in the codebase. |
| Market names name the TEAM, not a number (all sports) | [`applyTeamNumberLabel`](./services/api/src/lib/market-naming.ts), applied by `substituteTemplate` — which every market-name render goes through, so one rule covers the match page, list cards, the bet-slip leg label, bet history, ZillaFlash / ZillaBoost banners, push copy and the RiskZilla betticker. Fonbet names a per-team market by NUMBER, differently in each language — "Team 1 totals {threshold}", "Team Totals-1", "1 to win", "Инд. тоталы-1", "Победа 1" — and in a couple of captions its own `%1` / `%2` team placeholder leaks into the market name. Rendered verbatim that reads **"Team 1 totals 2.5" on a page showing Swansea vs Wrexham**: a market about exactly one of them that does not say which, and the same string is what the slip stores as the leg label, so the bettor cannot tell afterwards either. Number 1 is the home side and 2 the away side — the convention Fonbet's own factor ids use (`1` home / `2` away / `3` draw). Six anchored patterns, verified by rendering the entire live catalogue (2026-09-05): 198 distinct market-name shapes gain the team, and the near misses stay untouched — "1x2", "Score after 2 goals scored", "Score after 2 maps", "Score after 2 sets", "score in the series after 2 matches". Anything matching nothing is returned unchanged, so a caption Fonbet adds later degrades to today's wording rather than to a WRONG team. **Market names only**: `renderOutcomeLabel` deliberately calls the inner `substituteCore` instead, because an outcome cell sits under a header that already names the team and Fonbet writes some outcome captions mid-sentence ("Фрейм %P: инд. тотал-2 ударов Больше"), where swapping the number in reads as broken grammar — measured 0 of 25 096 outcome templates touched. A caller with no match in hand (the admin feed log, the backoffice market pickers) keeps the generic caption, which is the right label there. Esports never had this problem: Oddin marks the side structurally (`{side}` specifier, bare `home` / `away` outcome templates) and that path is unchanged — this is the same fix for a catalogue that numbers its teams instead of naming them. |
| Market scopes (the match page's tabs) | Grammar + derivation: [`packages/types/src/market-scope.ts`](./packages/types/src/market-scope.ts). Discovery for the backoffice: [`services/api/src/lib/fe-market-scopes.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/fe-market-scopes.ts). A scope is one tab on `/match/:id` AND the string stored in `fe_market_display_order.scope` / `fe_market_groups.scope`: `match`, `map_<N>`, `fb_<kinds>` (a Fonbet sub-event — `fb:400100/10100201` → `fb_400100_10100201`, with every per-player variant collapsed into `fb_players`), `top`, `custom_<key>`. **The label is not in the id**: Fonbet ships it as a prefix on the market-name template ("1st half corners: Match result"), so deriving a scope needs the template as well as the specifiers — which is why `deriveScope(specs)` (Match / Map N only) survives alongside `deriveMarketScope({specifiers, template})` for the callers that only ever see Oddin markets. Three consumers must agree byte-for-byte or the backoffice configures a tab the storefront never renders: the api's match-detail route, the web admin (which validates the scope in the URL and labels the tabs), and the DB CHECK constraints that mirror the regexes (migrations 0084, 0106). It lives in `@oddzilla/types` rather than `@oddzilla/db` for exactly one reason — apps/web needs it and must not depend on the db package; import it by subpath (`@oddzilla/types/market-scope`), never through the barrel, per the `oddzilla-types-barrel-imports` footgun. `@oddzilla/db` re-exports the predicates so schema consumers keep importing them from there. |
| Match-row component | [`apps/web/src/components/match/match-row.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/match/match-row.tsx) |
| Cookie notice banner + privacy policy | Store [`apps/web/src/lib/cookie-consent.ts`](./apps/web/src/lib/cookie-consent.ts) (localStorage `oz:cookie-consent` v2 — records only that the notice was dismissed; nothing is gated on it). Banner [`apps/web/src/components/shell/cookie-banner.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/shell/cookie-banner.tsx) — **purely informational** (operator decision 2026-07-02 for the UZ market): cookies, third-party embeds, and fe-analytics are ON BY DEFAULT; the banner's two "Accept all" buttons acknowledge and dismiss. Banner body + `/privacy` describe the on-by-default behaviour — keep them in sync with reality if the regime changes. **Guardrail: if a Reject / "Necessary only" control is ever reintroduced, it must genuinely disable the categories — a reject-labelled control that accepts is deceptive design; do not ship one.** Policy page [`apps/web/src/app/(main)/privacy/page.tsx`](./apps/web/src/app/(main)/privacy/page.tsx) (Privacy + Cookies Policy, English-only, points to browser-level cookie controls; operator-entity placeholders still to fill). The root layout pins `metadataBase` to `https://oddzilla.cc` so the emitted `og:image` / `twitter:image` URLs come out absolute; without it Next resolves them against `http://localhost:3000`, which is what production shipped until 2026-09-04 and why every link preview was broken), [`apps/mobile-android/app/src/main/res/drawable-xxxhdpi/{ic_launcher_foreground,ic_splash_logo}.png`](./apps/mobile-android/app/src/main/res/drawable-xxxhdpi/) (adaptive-icon foreground + splash bitmap; the `mipmap-anydpi-v26/ic_launcher.xml` and the splash theme entry in `values/themes.xml` resolve to these by name). Wordmark aspect (`WORDMARK_ASPECT` in `monogram.tsx`) must match the master's `width/height`; mobile top-bar width overrides in `globals.css` are derived from it. |

Inbound IDs are clamped to `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,128}$` at every layer so a hostile client can't poison log lines. |

[`web/WebViewHost.kt`](./apps/mobile-android/app/src/main/java/cc/oddzilla/app/web/WebViewHost.kt) hosts a single `WebView` pointing at `https://oddzilla.cc`; the web app is already responsive (`MobileBetSlipBar`, `MobileDrawersProvider`, `MobileShellOverlay`, mobile-first breakpoints in `apps/web/src/app/globals.css`) so wrapping it gives pixel parity with mobile Chrome. Cookies mirror from the WebView's `CookieManager` into [`PersistentCookieJar`](./apps/mobile-android/app/src/main/java/cc/oddzilla/app/data/api/CookieStore.kt) on every page-finished so the OkHttp client used by `DevicesRepository` (FCM device-register) authenticates against the same session. Version source-of-truth is `version.properties` at the project root; `scripts/release.ps1` (and the bash twin) builds a signed APK, hashes it, scps both the APK + new `version.json` to `team@178.104.174.24:/srv/oddzilla-apk/`. |

## Deep references

- [`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`](./docs/ARCHITECTURE.md) — service interaction, end-to-end flow walkthroughs (odds, bet placement, settlement, deposit, withdrawal), scale path, security boundaries
- [`docs/SCHEMA.md`](./docs/SCHEMA.md) — every table with columns, indexes, rationale, common queries
- [`docs/ODDIN.md`](./docs/ODDIN.md) — AMQP routing keys, XML shapes, REST endpoints, market IDs, recovery protocol
- [`docs/PHASES.md`](./docs/PHASES.md) — phase-by-phase roadmap with delivered detail per phase
- [`docs/OPERATIONS.md`](./docs/OPERATIONS.md) — deploy, env vars, backup, withdrawal admin runbook, incident playbook

## Conventions

- **One domain per file** in `packages/db/src/schema/`. No circular imports
  across packages (enforced by tsconfig project references).
- **Each Go service is its own module** (`services/<name>/go.mod`). Shared Go
  code goes in `services/<name>/internal/`. Do not create a shared Go module
  — duplicate small helpers to keep services independently deployable.
  Examples already duplicated: `oddinxml` (feed-ingester ↔ settlement),
  `amqp` consumer pattern (feed-ingester ↔ settlement).
- **Logs are JSON, structured.** TS uses `pino`, Go uses `zerolog`. Always
  include `service` and `event`/`component` fields.
- **Errors return early, never swallowed.** Go: wrap with
  `fmt.Errorf("context: %w", err)`. TS: throw typed errors from
  `services/api/src/lib/errors.ts`; Fastify maps them to HTTP status codes
  via the `setErrorHandler` in `server.ts`.
- **Tests live next to code.** `*.test.ts` and `*_test.go`. Integration
  tests hit the real Postgres container — never mock the database.
- **Commits:** imperative mood, lowercase, prefix by area
  (`db:`, `api:`, `feed:`, `web:`, `docs:`, `infra:`). No emojis.
- **Graceful idle.** Every service that needs external creds (Oddin AMQP,
  chain RPC) must boot cleanly when those creds are absent — log a warning
  and serve health only. Pattern is well-established in feed-ingester,
  settlement, wallet-watcher.
- **Docs stay in sync on every commit and deploy.** Before ANY commit
  lands (not just PR merges — direct commits too), check whether
  CLAUDE.md, the relevant `docs/*.md`, the root `README.md`, and any
  affected `services/*/README.md` still describe reality, and update
  them in the *same* commit or PR — never "in a follow-up". And before
  running `make deploy`, confirm the docs for everything being shipped
  are already up to date — a deploy of code whose docs are stale is an
  incomplete deploy. Stale docs are worse than missing ones: they
  mislead the next agent. Trigger map:
  - New service, data flow, or invariant → CLAUDE.md (`## Architecture
    map`, `## Invariants`, `## Where things live`) + `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`.
  - Schema migration → CLAUDE.md migration list under `## Where things
    live` + `docs/SCHEMA.md`.
  - Env var added / renamed / removed → `.env.example` + CLAUDE.md
    `## Local secrets that exist`.
  - Phase progress, new component shipped, acceptance bar reached →
    `docs/PHASES.md` + the CLAUDE.md `## Live phase status` table.
  - Deploy / backup / runbook / incident step changed →
    `docs/OPERATIONS.md`.
  - Oddin protocol detail changed → `docs/ODDIN.md`.
  - Service-internal change (new module, renamed binary, added flag) →
    that service's `services/<name>/README.md`.
  - File or symbol renamed / removed → grep `CLAUDE.md README.md docs/`
    and the service READMEs for the old name and either update or
    delete the references.

  If a doc claim is no longer true, fix it in the same merge. When
  reviewing, treat "diff touches code but no doc" as a smell — ask
  whether one of the triggers above applies before approving. The same
  smell applies at deploy time: if `make deploy-status` shows commits
  whose docs were never checked, do the doc pass before deploying.

## Hard limits

- **Don't expose Postgres or Redis publicly.** Compose binds them to
  `127.0.0.1` only; Caddy never fronts them.
- **Don't bypass bet-delay** when a user has `bet_delay_seconds > 0`. The
  admin toggle is the only legitimate way to skip it.
- **Don't return secrets in API responses** — including internal user fields
  like `password_hash`, `refresh_token_hash`, KYC raw documents, and
  `derivation_path` from `deposit_addresses`.
- **Don't run SQL migrations manually on prod.** Use `make migrate`; it's
  transactional and records history in `_migrations`.
- **Don't add a service without adding a healthcheck to compose.** A service
  with no `/healthz` will fail silently.
- **The signer container is the only place `HD_MASTER_MNEMONIC` lives.**
  PR #132 moved derivation out of the API into [`services/signer/`](./services/signer/).
  The API talks to it over a Unix socket on a tmpfs volume; no
  other container mounts it. Putting `HD_MASTER_MNEMONIC` back into
  the API's env (or compose service entry) defeats the entire isolation
  layer — if you find yourself needing to, talk to the signer instead.
  The signer also calls `os.Unsetenv("HD_MASTER_MNEMONIC")` at boot so
  `/proc/<pid>/environ` is uninteresting after startup.
- **The web tier carries no server secrets.** The docker-compose `*web-base`
  anchor intentionally has NO `env_file: .env`; each web replica lists only
  the non-secret runtime vars it reads (grep `apps/web/src` for
  `process.env`). JWTs are HS256 (symmetric), so a web RCE able to read
  `JWT_SECRET` from `process.env` could FORGE admin tokens — the escalation
  `apps/web/src/lib/auth.ts` documents as foreclosed. Never add `env_file:
  .env` (or any secret) to a web service. Verify with `docker compose exec
  web1 env | grep -E 'JWT_SECRET|POSTGRES_PASSWORD|ODDIN_TOKEN'` → nothing.
- **Linking a deposit sending-address requires proof of control.**
  `POST /wallet/addresses` verifies an EIP-191 signature (challenge from
  `GET /wallet/addresses/challenge`, binding userId+address+issuedAt) that
  recovers the claimed address, plus a CEX hot-wallet blocklist. Without it
  a bettor could register an address they don't own — e.g. an exchange hot
  wallet every withdrawal originates from — and the wallet-watcher would
  auto-credit those deposits to them. Don't drop the signature check.
- **Don't disable the audit-log trigger.** `admin_audit_log_chain_trg`
  computes `prev_hash` + `row_hash` on every INSERT under an advisory
  lock. Bypassing it (e.g. via `ALTER TABLE … DISABLE TRIGGER`) breaks
  the chain at every later row, and the verifier (`admin_audit_chain_check`)
  will flag every subsequent insert as tampered.
- **Don't bypass the CSRF plugin.** Every state-changing API request
  (POST / PUT / PATCH / DELETE) must carry an `Origin` or `Referer` in
  `CORS_ORIGINS`. Server-to-server callers (the Next.js middleware's
  `/auth/refresh` fetch, the web container's `/auth/me` lookup) set
  `origin` explicitly. Browsers always set Origin on POST. If you find
  yourself adding a route that needs to skip the gate, surface the use
  case first — almost certainly you want a webhook signature instead.

## Live phase status

What's actually wired up vs scaffolded vs deferred. Last updated after the
post-Phase-8 Oddin-workflow hardening pass; production stack is live at
`team@178.104.174.24` consuming the integration AMQPS feed.

| Component | Status | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Fonbet sports feed (`fonbet-ingester`) | Local stack verified (2026-09-03); review fixes 2026-09-04; moved to fon.bet in English 2026-09-04. **Feed off by default (`FONBET_ENABLED`), and settlement off by default INDEPENDENTLY (`FONBET_SETTLE_ENABLED=false`) — run the feed alone first, soak the grader against Fonbet's own results on staging, then enable settlement** | Polls `events/list` (`fon.bet`, `lang=en`, scopeMarket 1600) every 5 s, diffs against the previous snapshot and writes only deltas into `markets` / `market_outcomes` / `odds_history` + `odds.raw`; sub-events (halves, corners, player props) ride the `variant` specifier, esports maps `map=N`; market / outcome labels come from Fonbet's `factorsCatalog/tables` in the feed language plus every other locale in `descriptionLangs` (en + ru), so the storefront's `/ru` keeps Russian market names off an English feed. Staleness watchdog + SIGTERM suspend the whole Fonbet catalog (`fb:` URNs only). Settlement: the ingester grades closed matches from Fonbet's results feed (1X2 / double chance / handicap / total, halves, periods, statistic rows; conservative sport rules) and hands them to `services/settlement` over the `settlement.external` Redis stream (`internal/extstream` → `Settler.ApplyExternalSettlement`), so tickets on Fonbet markets settle through the same apply-once path as Oddin's. Markets the rules skip (overtime tables, exotic shapes, unsupported sports) stay open for manual settlement. odds-publisher was batched in the same change (~5k ticks/s). **Review fixes (2026-09-04):** `settlement.external` consumer recreates its group on `NOGROUP` and creates it from `0` (invariant 7); `odds.raw` stays at the shared 100k MAXLEN and the ingester paces its cold-start republish on odds-publisher's group lag instead (`OddsBacklog`, high-water 50k); `settlement.external` capped at 20k; `FONBET_SETTLE_ENABLED` defaults to false; the results-feed fallback match requires home-before-away (an order-blind substring match bound mirrored fixtures and inverted every grade); overtime is folded into a two-way score exactly once (`scoreFor` reports `otApplied`, `breakTie` honours it); `emitted` is stamped only after the settle message is on the stream; urls.json hosts are adopted only when `https` and under a domain from the operator's static lists; `EnsureSport` retries on a `sports_slug_key` collision with an `-fb-<id>` suffix; `FONBET_MAX_MATCHES` no longer neuters the shrink guard (`Snapshot.TotalMatches`) nor closes capped matches (`Snapshot.Capped`); compose gives the service `mem_limit: 768m` / `cpus: 1.0` / `GOMEMLIMIT=640MiB` (it holds the whole line plus a fresh snapshot in RAM — the anchor's 320m was never exercised under a cgroup); `ODDS_HISTORY_SKIP_PMID_MIN` is the operator brake on `odds_history` growth. **fon.bet in English (2026-09-04):** the feed read the Kazakhstan site in Russian, which is why traditional-sport market names arrived in Russian on the storefront. Now `fon.bet` + `FONBET_LANG=en` by default, with `FONBET_SITE_ORIGIN` / `FONBET_LOGO_CDN` added so the request `Origin` and the asset CDN follow the site instead of being hard-coded. Identity-neutral (same event / market / outcome ids — see the Where-things-live row); the substantive part was teaching the settlement grader English, since it reads Fonbet's own text. **Runtime switch (migration 0099, 2026-09-04):** the **Fonbet feed** card on `/admin/feed` turns the feed on/off without a restart — `feed_control.fonbet_enabled` (NULL = follow `FONBET_ENABLED`), read every 2 s by the ingester's switch watcher in [`main.go`](./services/fonbet-ingester/cmd/fonbet-ingester/main.go) (`switchWatcher` / `runFeed`); OFF = `SuspendAll` + stop polling + settlement worker paused, ON = boot the feed in place; a boot failure while ON retries every 30 s instead of `log.Fatal` (which would crashloop under the switch). The card reads `GET /admin/feed/fonbet-status` (switch + `fonbet_applied_*` ack from Postgres, live counters from the Redis hash `fonbet:feed:status` the service refreshes every 5 s). |
| DB schema + migrations | Live | 30 migrations 0000–0029. See "SQL migrations" row above for the full list. | Catalog API + sport/match SSR pages | Live | `/catalog/sports` filters `active=true` (hides `unclassified`); `/catalog/sports/:slug` returns inline match-winner odds per row (paired by Oddin outcome_id: `"1"`=home, `"2"`=away) and accepts `?tournament=N` and/or `?team=N` (competitor id; scoped to this sport, returns the team name as `filteredTeam` so the storefront chip can render even when the team has zero current matches) to filter the list; `/catalog/sports/:slug/tournaments` returns tournaments under a sport with live+upcoming match counts; `/catalog/matches/:id` returns all active (`status=1`) markets — provider-id whitelist removed. Odds rendered at up to 4dp with trailing zeros trimmed to a ≥2dp minimum — preserves Oddin's native precision (1.003 stays 1.003) instead of truncating to 2dp. **The storefront must render through [`formatOddsDisplay`](./packages/types/src/odds.ts)**: the API payload has carried 4dp since 2026-05-25, but every price *display* still did `toFixed(2)` until 2026-08-27, so a genuine 1.003 reached the user as "1.00" — a price that doesn't exist, and indistinguishable from a real 1.0000. Three server-side twins run the same floor-and-trim algorithm (`formatOdds` here, `formatOddsTrim` in `bettor-odds-adjustment.ts`, and the Go `formatPublishedOdds` in odds-publisher); keep them identical or the displayed price and the drift reference diverge and bets get rejected for phantom drift. List/count endpoints (`/catalog/matches`, `/catalog/sports/:slug`, `/catalog/sports/:slug/tournaments`, `/catalog/search`, `/catalog/live-counts`) gate on the shared `hasActiveMarket` predicate — at least one `markets.status=1` row AND `matches.status IN ('not_started','live')` — so matches with nothing to bet on (or that already went terminal) are dropped. The match-winner can be briefly suspended (mid-round, post-goal) while secondary markets stay active, and the row still surfaces. Defense in depth on the storefront side complements the upstream lifecycle fixes (terminal-status apply on `odds_change`, `bet_stop` blanket suspension, `bet_settlement certainty=2`); phantom-drain remains as the safety net for matches Oddin never sends a terminal signal for. Inline odds are additionally restricted to `variant='way:two'` (`isTwoWayMatchWinner`) so 1X2 sports like eFootball don't render misleading 1/2 buttons; the match still appears in the list and clicking through shows the full 1X2 on `/match/:id`. |

Each tournament links to `/sport/:slug?tournament=ID`; the sport page reads the param server-side and shows a dismissible filter chip. **Rows keep a shared left edge without inventing a mark** (2026-09-05): Fonbet has a competition logo for only about half its leagues, so a bucket is normally a mix of logo'd and bare rows, and `TournamentLogoMark` used to return `null` — which pulled the bare names ~20px left of their neighbours and made the list read as ragged. `CategoryGroup` now computes `reserveLogoSlot` from the data (any row in the group has a `logoUrl`) and the mark holds an empty 14px square for the rest; a group with no logos at all reserves nothing, keeping the full 240px for names. Fonbet's categories are real (country / competition, from the segment-name prefix: `"Spain. Primera Division. Season 26/27"` → `Spain`), which is what the grouping is for — before it, football showed 50-odd dotted tournament names in one flat column with no country structure. `groupTournamentsByCategory` in [`sidebar.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/shell/sidebar.tsx) keeps the endpoint's tournament order inside each bucket. **Navigation rework (2026-09-04)** after the first Fonbet-era look at a real basketball tree (18 tournaments across 12 countries, all flat, all expanded): (1) every sport row carries a **disclosure caret** that opens its tournament tree in place — before this the tree existed only while you were ON `/sport/:slug`, so comparing two sports' offers meant navigating twice; expansion state is per-slug in the sidebar, the active sport still auto-expands, and the fetch effect now pulls the list for every open slug (guarded by a `requestedSlugs` ref, not state, so StrictMode's double-invoke can't double-fetch). (2) Labelled category buckets are **collapsed by default** and sort by **name**, not live count — with the header now a click target, a target that reorders itself as matches go live is worse than one occasionally below the fold. **Operator ordering landed 2026-09-05 (migration 0103):** a category pinned on `/admin/categories` carries a `display_order` and heads its sport's tree in that sequence, so Football opens on England rather than Albania; everything unpinned stays alphabetical behind it, which is byte-identical to the previous behaviour for a sport nobody has touched. `groupTournamentsByCategory` sorts pin-then-name, with `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER` standing in for an unpinned bucket the same way the tournament sort already does for a null `risk_tier`. The bucket holding the active `?tournament=` filter auto-opens (via an effect that only ever forces `open` true, so the caret can still close it). **The header is two controls, not one (2026-09-04, migration 0102):** the caret collapses, and the LABEL links to `/sport/:slug?category=<id>`, filtering the sport page to the whole bucket. They were a single toggle button until list-excluded categories landed — a bucket the match lists deliberately skip has to be reachable, and "expand it, then pick one of seventeen tournaments" is not reachable in any useful sense. Splitting them pays off on the ordinary country buckets too: one click on `England` is now every English league at once. The hover surface still spans the whole row so the two halves read as one header, the bucket auto-opens when it IS the active category, and a bucket flagged `hidden_from_lists` wears a quiet `EyeOff` mark — without it a bettor has nothing to explain why Football's badge reads 13 while this one bucket reads 10. The unlabelled bucket has no header and stays open, so esports look exactly as they did. (3) Category headers get a **country flag** — see the flag-proxy row below — plus normal 12px `--fg` text instead of 9.5px letterspaced uppercase `--fg-dim`, which was failing on contrast. A category that is NOT a country (cycling races, `NBA 2K26`, `Friendly games`) falls back to the bucket's own tournament logo, and to **no mark at all** when there isn't one. The first cut put a two-letter initials disc there so every row kept the same left edge; on the cycling tree that stamped `TO` / `VE` / `ZT` beside Tour of Britain, Vuelta Espana and ZLM Tour — invented monograms abbreviating words already on the row — so it was dropped (2026-09-04). The bucket logo is taken only when every tournament in it that HAS a logo has the SAME one (`TournamentGroup.logoUrl`), which is true of a race and its stages and false of a country holding several leagues; the caret still holds the left edge. (4) Indentation was **reduced**, not added: the tree steps in 14+8px (was 22+10) and tournaments inside a category 10px (was 8 on top of the deeper base), because on a 240px panel every pixel of indent is a pixel off the tournament name. (4b) The **Sports** nav item was removed 2026-09-05: it existed to give the Fonbet traditional line one entry point back when the rail was a single esports-first list, and the Sports / Esports switch a few pixels below is both a better entry point and the one bettors reach for — two controls competing for one job. The `/sports` route still exists; its `shell.sportsNav` message key is now unused in all six locales. (5) The list splits into **Sports** and **Esports** (Sports on the left since 2026-09-05 — render order only; `esport` is still the tab a fresh visitor lands on and the stored preference key is unchanged, so nobody's saved side flipped), switched by a side-by-side segmented control keyed off `sports.kind` rather than stacked sections — stacked, the Fonbet traditional line always sat below ~16 esports, so reaching Football was a scroll on every visit. The tab persists in `localStorage["oz:sport-tab"]` (read in an effect, never the initializer, or SSR and hydration disagree) and follows the page: landing on `/sport/football` while the rail shows Esports would hide the very row the user is looking at. The control's token pair is `--bg-sunken` track / `--surface-2` active pill, the only pair that stays *raised* in both themes — the obvious `--surface-2` / `--bg-elevated` inverts on dark (`#1a1a1c` track, `#131314` pill) and reads as a recess. With only one kind on offer (a feed outage, Fonbet switched off) it degrades to a plain label rather than a toggle with a dead half. Edit mode follows the SAME tab, and keeps the tab strip live so the other half is one click away: it used to render one flat list of every sport, so on a 16-esport estate reordering Football meant scrolling past every esport, and the arrows on that row moved it through sports that are never on screen together. The saved preference is still one global slug list — `move` swaps the two rows at their positions in the full visible order, so the other kind keeps its slots and "up" means "above the next sport you can actually see". The edit-mode universe is visible + hidden sports, so a bettor who hid every traditional sport can still reach that tab to un-hide one. |

So flags need no migration and no per-category upload. **Proxied, not hot-linked**: Fonbet is a scraped public line, not a data partner, so pointing every visitor's browser at their CDN would put our whole audience in their logs and leave the flags one referer rule from breaking; the api fetches once and caches the bytes in Redis (30 d, `allkeys-lru` so an eviction just costs a refetch), serving them `immutable`. Misses are negative-cached 24 h, but an upstream *failure* deliberately is not — a transient blip must not blank the flags for a day. CDN host follows `FONBET_LOGO_CDN`, the same var fonbet-ingester uses for team crests, so a fon.bet ↔ fonbet.kz estate move needs no storefront rebuild. **The upstream file names are not a clean transform** (`Costa-Rica` but `Czech_Republic`, `South-Korea-2` for Korea, `Madagaskar` for Madagascar), so the map is an explicit 200-entry table verified by probing the live CDN rather than a string rewrite; anything absent (esports categories, `Other`, `WC 2026`) resolves to null and costs no request — the sidebar then falls back to the bucket's own tournament logo, or to no mark at all. |

| Web SSR replica fan-out + request-id tracing | Live (2026-05-11) | Storefront runs three Next.js replicas (`web1` / `web2` / `web3`) behind Caddy `lb_policy least_conn` with active health checks on `/healthz` (5 s interval; replica drops out within 7 s if it goes down). `web1` always runs; `web2` + `web3` are gated on the `scaled` Compose profile so local dev keeps the single-process behaviour. Per-replica `mem_limit` is 400 MiB (down from the previous single-replica 640) so the three together fit cleanly in 8 GB alongside postgres + api + workers. Rolling deploys via `make recreate-web` cycle the replicas serially and wait for each healthcheck, so updates never take all three down at once. Cross-process tracing rides an `x-request-id` header: the Next.js middleware ([apps/web/src/middleware.ts](./apps/web/src/middleware.ts)) generates or echoes one per page render and forwards it on every server-side fetch (`lib/server-fetch.ts`, `lib/auth.ts`); Fastify's `genReqId` picks it up so `reqId` lands on every api log line; ws-gateway logs it on upgrade; the api echoes it back to the browser via `onSend`. A single grep with the request id finds the SSR render + every API call + WS upgrade it triggered (`make weblogs` interleaves all three replicas; the production-side jq recipe is in [docs/OPERATIONS.md](./docs/OPERATIONS.md#logs)). |

| Recovery flow | Live | `POST /v1/{product}/recovery/initiate_request` triggered on every (re)connect for both producers + on `alive subscribed=0` + on `alive` timestamp drift > 5s. **Suspend-before-recover is unconditional (2026-05-11):** every AMQP (re)connect runs `store.FlushAndSuspendActiveCatalog` BEFORE asking Oddin to replay — flips every active market on a `not_started`/`live` match to `status=-1`, moves every `live` MATCH to `status='suspended'` so the match leaves the offer with its markets (suspending markets alone left matches asserting `live` with nothing to walk the claim back — 675 such rows had piled up by 2026-09-03, oldest from 2026-04-18; only `live` is moved, and `UpdateMatchStatus` carries a narrow exception letting `suspended` return to `not_started`), and nulls outcome `published_odds`/`raw_odds`/`probability`. The replay re-activates only what Oddin re-confirms; anything Oddin omits stays suspended and drops from listings via the catalog filter. Rationale: even a few seconds of feed gap can leave a market quoting odds Oddin already moved off of, and a single placement at stale odds is real-money loss. The 2026-05-09 disk-full incident wedged 33 matches this way (Oddin's replay didn't carry their terminal status because they were no longer in Oddin's active state by reconnect time). The auto-path does SUSPEND only — no DELETE — because the bulk DELETE races with concurrent settlement INSERTs on `settlements.market_id` (no `ON DELETE` action) and trips an FK violation that aborts the whole tx; SUSPEND is race-safe because UPDATE on the parent doesn't trigger child FK checks, and the catalog filter gates on `markets.status=1` so a suspended row is invisible whether or not its parent match still exists. The recovery request itself clamps the cursor to `now - 24 h` (per `handler.recoveryWindowCap`) to stay in Oddin's lenient rate-limit tier (< 30 min) and bound replay latency; anything older falls through as "stayed suspended". Admin `POST /admin/feed/recovery` (with `flushOdds=true` default) now uses the SAME suspend-based flush as the auto-path — it `UPDATE`s every active `not_started`/`live` market to `status=-1` and nulls outcome odds/probability, then rewinds the cursor + `pg_notify('feed_recovery')`, all in one transaction (atomic: no half-flush, no rewound-cursor-without-replay). It used to `DELETE FROM markets` first, but that bulk delete deadlocks against the live feed-ingester's concurrent upserts (the "something went wrong" 500 an operator hit on 2026-06-01) and FK-races settlement INSERTs — so the DELETE was dropped entirely. The flush is wrapped in a `lock_timeout`-bounded retry (`RECOVERY_MAX_ATTEMPTS`) and returns a typed 503 `recovery_lock_contended` instead of an unhandled 500 if the feed stays too busy. Storefront end-state is identical (a `status=-1` market is invisible to the catalog filter, same as a deleted one). Stale market rows Oddin no longer re-confirms remain as invisible suspended rows (same as the auto-path; physical row cleanup, if ever needed, belongs in a feed-ingester sweeper, not the API). **Alive watchdog (2026-05-29):** suspend-before-recover only fires on a *successful* (re)connect, so it cannot help when the token is revoked server-side — every reconnect fails, `OnConnect` never runs, and the catalog keeps quoting frozen odds (the 2026-05-29 token-revocation incident: ~8k markets stayed bettable for over an hour with the feed dead). The time-based `runAliveWatchdog` (in [`services/feed-ingester/cmd/feed-ingester/main.go`](./services/feed-ingester/cmd/feed-ingester/main.go)) closes that gap: it reads `lastAmqpMessageUnix` (bumped on EVERY delivery — alive heartbeat, odds_change, anything) and when no message has arrived for `FEED_STALE_SUSPEND_SECONDS` (default 20s = two missed ~10s alives) it runs `FlushAndSuspendActiveCatalog` + broadcasts `marketStatus=-1` frames, independent of connection state. Suspends once per silence episode (guarded), clears the guard when data resumes; re-activation rides the existing recovery paths. `<=0` disables it. **The earlier `handleAlive` gap-detection only acts on alives that *arrive* — it never fired when alives stopped entirely; the watchdog is the missing absence-detector.** |
| Wedged matches admin tool | Live (2026-05-11) | `/admin/wedged-matches` lists matches at `not_started` more than 1 h past `scheduled_at` with active markets (the same shape the suspend-before-recover flush is designed to clean up automatically). Each row exposes Refresh from REST → `pg_notify('fixture_refresh', urn)`; feed-ingester re-fetches via `RefreshFromFixture` and applies the fixture body's `status` attribute, which carries Oddin's authoritative current state. Routes in [`services/api/src/modules/admin/wedged-matches.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/wedged-matches.ts), page at [`apps/web/src/app/admin/wedged-matches/`](./apps/web/src/app/admin/wedged-matches/). |
| Storefront catalog time-gate | Live (2026-05-11) | `hasActiveMarket` in `catalog/routes.ts` adds a 6 h time gate on `not_started` matches: a row scheduled > 6 h ago that never transitioned to `live` is broken data (live esports rounds don't run that long), so the listing endpoints drop it even if a stray market row stayed at status=1. Defense in depth on top of the suspend-before-recover flush — if the structural fix ever regresses, users still don't see fossilised "upcoming" matches from days ago. |
| `<sport_event_status>` is the lifecycle source of truth | Live (2026-05-06) | The Oddin spec (§2.4) lists exactly 8 message types: `fixture_change`, `odds_change`, `alive`, `bet_cancel`, `rollback_bet_cancel`, `bet_settlement`, `rollback_bet_settlement`, `snapshot_complete`. **Neither `match_status_change` nor `bet_stop` is in the protocol** — the integration broker for bookmaker 142 never emits them; our `handleMatchStatusChange` and `handleBetStop` handlers stay wired but only as a graceful no-op for any bookmaker that did emit them. The authoritative lifecycle signal is `<sport_event_status status="…"/>` carried inside every `odds_change` (per §2.4.1.2: documented values are `0` not_started, `1` live, `4` closed, `5` cancelled). `handleOddsChange` forwards every observed code through `store.UpdateMatchStatus`, whose SQL guard rejects regressions (terminal → anything else, anything → `not_started`). Net effect: a match appears on the live offer the first odds_change it ships with `status=1`, and drops off the offer the odds_change it ships with `status=4` or `5`. |
| All-markets-terminal close | Live (2026-05-06) | Per spec §2.4.2: "When a market or market line is settled with a bet_settlement message, it will be automatically removed from all subsequent match odds_change messages." So once the last market settles, no further odds_change arrives — a final `<sport_event_status status="4"/>` may never land. Settlement now calls `store.MarkMatchClosedIfAllMarketsTerminal(event_urn)` after every `bet_settlement` and every `bet_cancel`. The single SQL flips `matches.status='closed'` only when no market on the match is at `1 / 0 / -1 / -2` — so per-map market settlements during a live match (where the match-winner is still active) don't false-positive. Forward-only via the same status guard. |
| Phantom-drain ticker | **Removed** (2026-05-06, PR #122) | Was a 15-min REST sweep that mopped up matches whose `matches.status` never advanced. Removed because (a) `<sport_event_status>` inside every `odds_change` is the authoritative lifecycle signal post-PR #121, and (b) the drain was masking real Oddin / ingest bugs by silently overwriting rows whose in-band path had failed. A drifting match is now visible — debug it. |
| Per-message outcome diff cleanup | Live (2026-05-25) | Per Oddin / UOF protocol, each `odds_change` carries the FULL current outcome set for the markets it references — outcomes Oddin omits are no longer on offer. The previous code only upserted the outcomes IN the message and left omitted rows at their stale `active=true, published_odds=…`. Confirmed production bug: a "Winning margin 5-7" stayed quoted at 2.15 on /match/707005 long after Oddin had pulled it from offer. Fix lives in [`store.DeactivateMissingOutcomes`](./services/feed-ingester/internal/store/markets.go) (one UNNEST anti-join per message), called from `handleOddsChange` right after `UpsertOutcomesBulk` and scoped to status=1 markets only (suspended/handover/terminal markets are gated by `markets.status` upstream and stripping their last-quoted odds would make the routine mid-round "Suspended" rendering look broken). Defense in depth: bet placement re-reads `market_outcomes.active` and rejects with `outcome_not_active`, so even if a live WS subscriber hasn't refreshed, a stale slip click fails at the boundary. |
| Live-stream embeds (Twitch / YouTube / Kick / Gjirafa) | Live (2026-05-06) | Oddin's `/v1/sports/{lang}/sport_events/{matchURN}/fixture` returns a `<tv_channels><tv_channel name=… language=… stream_url=…/></tv_channels>` block per the public schema. `oddinxml.FixtureTvChannelsList` parses it; resolver `merge()` JSON-encodes the rows into `matches.tv_channels` (migration 0022). Fixture `STREAM_URL` change_type 106 now also triggers `RefreshFromFixture` so the column tracks broadcaster swaps mid-event. **Belt-and-suspenders:** feed-ingester's `handleOddsChange` also fires `pg_notify('fixture_refresh', urn)` on every not_started → live transition because Oddin frequently attaches broadcasters around match start *without* emitting the 106 event — the existing 5-min per-URN cooldown in `runFixtureRefreshListener` keeps repeated live ticks from hammering Oddin's REST. **That transition refresh is necessary but NOT sufficient, and on its own it caught almost nothing** (fixed 2026-08-31): it fires seconds after kickoff, and Oddin attaches broadcasters at or *after* that moment, so the fetch returns a fixture with no `<tv_channels>` and — with the 106 event not arriving in practice — nothing ever asked again. Measured on production before the fix: **649 of 724 live matches had NULL `tv_channels`, and ZERO of 523 upcoming matches had any**, while Oddin's fixture endpoint was serving three channels for a match stored as NULL; a single manual `pg_notify` populated it, proving the parse + persist paths were fine and this was purely a timing race. The fix is `runStreamBackfillSweeper` in [`main.go`](./services/feed-ingester/cmd/feed-ingester/main.go) — every 60 s it selects live matches with NULL `tv_channels` whose `live_started_at` is between 1 and 15 minutes ago ([`SelectLiveMatchesMissingStreams`](./services/feed-ingester/internal/store/catalog.go), batch of 40) and re-emits `fixture_refresh` for each, riding the same listener cooldown. Both bounds are load-bearing: the 1-minute floor skips the moment the transition already covered, and the 15-minute ceiling stops us re-asking forever about the many matches that genuinely have no broadcaster. `/catalog/matches/:id` classifies each entry into `{platform: twitch \| youtube \| kick \| gjirafa \| vpplayer \| other, embedId, url, name, language}` and surfaces it as `match.streams`. **`vpplayer` is `host.vpplayer.tech`, Gjirafa's white-label player host** — Oddin labels those channels "Gjirafa" but serves them from that domain, not `video.gjirafa.com`, so before 2026-08-31 they classified as `other` and silently never embedded (one of three advertised sources on a live eFootball match). It is the only platform with a null `embedId`: the URL already IS the player page (`/player/<account>/<video>.html`) and goes into the iframe verbatim, so the api shape-checks the path before classifying it. Needs its own CSP `frame-src` entry. The storefront `apps/web/src/components/match/match-streams.tsx` renders an iframe (Twitch via `https://player.twitch.tv/?channel=…&parent=<host>`, YouTube via `youtube-nocookie.com/embed/…`, Kick via `https://player.kick.com/<channel>`, Gjirafa via `https://video.gjirafa.com/embed/<slug>`) with a tab strip when there are multiple feeds. Anything that doesn't classify as embeddable is hidden (no outbound-link row) — only when the match has zero embeddable feeds at all does a single fallback card link out. The CSP `frame-src` (emitted by Next.js middleware in [`apps/web/src/middleware.ts`](./apps/web/src/middleware.ts), not Caddy) whitelists `https://player.twitch.tv https://www.twitch.tv https://www.youtube.com https://www.youtube-nocookie.com https://player.kick.com https://video.gjirafa.com`. |
| Auto-mapping (sport/category/tournament/match) | Live | REST-driven via `GET /v1/sports/en/sport_events/{urn}/fixture`; falls back to placeholder under default sport's auto category on 404; mapping_review_queue rows for everything created |
| Pre-match → live `-2` handover sweeper | Live | feed-ingester ticks every 15s; demotes markets stuck at -2 for >60s to -1 (suspended) |
| Settlement: settle / rollback_settle / cancel / rollback_cancel | Live | Apply-once via `(event_urn, market_id, specifiers_hash, type, payload_hash)` 5-tuple |

| Admin: withdrawals approve flow | Live | `/admin/withdrawals` page with approve/reject/mark-submitted/confirmed/failed. **4-eyes (security audit PR #130)**: the actor that confirms must differ from the actor that approved (`approver_cannot_confirm` 403; DB-level CHECK as defence-in-depth). `tx_hash` validated per network (ERC20 `^0x[0-9a-fA-F]{64}$`, TRC20 `^(0x)?[0-9a-fA-F]{64}$` normalised to no-prefix), unique partial index on `(network, tx_hash)`. mark-confirmed pre-checks `balance >= debit`. Reject restricted to `requested` only (use mark-failed for already-approved rows); user-cancel and admin-reject / fail now write a `wallet_ledger` audit row (`adjustment` / `withdrawal_cancel` / `withdrawal_reject` / `withdrawal_fail`) for the lock release. |

Shared `CreateUserForm` is parameterized by `mode` — `admin` mode exposes role choice and drops bettor-only knobs (bet-delay + stake limit). |
